# Investigation question bank: what the project answers (as built)

Assessment of every question in the team's "Loan Rangers master investigation question bank" against the project as built: this repo (code, config, docs, policy register and indexed public policy sources, eval reports), PR #1-#9 descriptions (PR #10 changes are in the code), the Oct 14, 2026 handoff document and the run reports. Status uses the question bank's own scheme: ANSWERED / PARTIALLY ANSWERED / UNKNOWN / NOT APPLICABLE.

**Scope and date (read this first).** This is a dated investigation: it assesses the project as it stood on **October 15, 2026**, around PR #10, and is not a claim that every row describes the current repository. PRs #17 (synthetic bureau service), #18 (optional Langfuse observability), #20 (Ruff pre-commit hook) and #21 (CI workflow) merged later and are only partly reflected in the original assessment. The before/after metrics in `docs/investigation-question-bank-before-after.md` are historical and are not recomputed. Rows corrected by hand after review are listed in the CSV `flags` column as `manual_correction`: Q263, Q449, Q451, Q543, Q614, Q669, Q848, Q856, Q857, Q863 and Q952.

**How this was produced (read before relying on it).** Each question was matched (BM25) to the project materials; gpt-oss-120b drafted a status, a 1-2 line answer, the evidence and a verbatim quote. A rule check rejects any answer whose quote is not found word-for-word in the cited material (downgraded to UNKNOWN). Qwen3-VL-8B (a different model family) then checked that the cited evidence supports the answer; an unsupported verdict is downgraded one level. Nothing here is human-reviewed yet; treat every row as a pointer to its evidence, not as a ruling. Answers describe the PROTOTYPE: the four underwriting thresholds are synthetic, never real lender policy, and Bajaj Finance is a public reference lender, not a partner. UNKNOWN rows name who has to answer (VaaniLabs, a partner lender, real applicants, or the team).

**Markdown vs CSV.** The Markdown is the post-downgrade rendered view; except for the hand-corrected rows below, the CSV keeps the assessor's earlier answers, evidence and flags (for example `quote_not_verbatim` and `judge_downgrade`). The two files therefore share all 1,040 question texts but differ in some answer/evidence fields: after the hand corrections below, 67 answers and 248 evidence fields still differ, all on rows the Markdown shows as UNKNOWN. Rows marked `manual_correction` were corrected by hand in both files and are excluded from those counts.

**Run-to-run stability (read this).** The same assessment was run twice on identical materials and settings. 743 of 1040 questions (71%) got the same status both times; the replicate scored ANSWERED 56.6% and ANSWERED+PARTIAL 67.7%. So a single row's status is a pointer, and section-level differences of a few questions are within noise. The CSV has both runs (`status_after` and `status_replicate`).

**Totals (1040 questions):** ANSWERED: 583 (56.1%), PARTIALLY ANSWERED: 129 (12.4%), UNKNOWN: 317 (30.5%), NOT APPLICABLE: 11 (1.1%)

**Who must answer the 317 UNKNOWN questions** (a question can name more than one): the project team: 265, VaaniLabs: 172, a partner lender: 58, real applicants (user research): 10. 194 of them need someone outside the team. (Who-must-answer is the assessor's label, normalised; it is not checked.)

Before/after: see `docs/investigation-question-bank-before-after.md`. "Before" = the same assessment over the project as it stood before the question-bank work (no indexed policy-source chunks, no research notes from this pass); "after" = the current branch including `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md`, `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md`, `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` and the indexed public policy sources.

Human handoff (s.22); policy source BFL-PRIVACY (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/privacy-policy> |

## 2. USER JOURNEY

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 31 | What happens to an applicant from the moment they submit a loan application until the final decision? | ANSWERED | After submission the application is in the SUBMITTED state, the system transitions it to UNDER_REVIEW, runs the ZEN underwriting engine and then moves to APPROVED or REJECTED (or to INFO_REQUESTED/MANUAL_REVIEW for missing or contradictory data) before a final decision is recorded. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 32 | What happens immediately after rejection? | ANSWERED | Immediately after a rejection, the applicant is sent an SMS on their registered mobile number informing them of the rejection. | `data/sources/policy_register.yaml`; policy source BFL-FAQ-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/contact-us-faq-queries> |
| 33 | How is the rejection currently communicated? | ANSWERED | Rejection is communicated to the applicant via an SMS sent to their registered mobile number. | policy source BFL-FAQ-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/contact-us-faq-queries> |
| 34 | Through which channels is rejection communicated? | ANSWERED | Rejection is communicated via an SMS sent to the applicant’s registered mobile number. | `data/sources/policy_register.yaml`; policy source BFL-FAQ-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/contact-us-faq-queries> |
| 35 | Does the applicant receive a reason code? | PARTIAL | The prototype defines reason codes for each rule and marks them as customer‑visible, but the materials do not explicitly state that the applicant is presented with those codes in the user journey. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule MIN_MONTHLY_INCOME (reason code ERR_INCOME_BELOW_MINIMUM); `decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`) |
| 36 | Does the applicant receive a human-readable reason? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system returns a human‑readable decision card that shows the outcome and a per‑rule table with reason codes and explanations for the applicant. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/facts.py`; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B) |
| 37 | Does the applicant receive multiple reasons? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 38 | Does the applicant receive information about how to address those reasons? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system provides the applicant with a concrete improvement plan that explains how to address each failed rule. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § 1. Project objective and product definition; memory: project note (loan-advisory-agent) § Phases 1-4 (Oct 2026) |

Project objective and product definition; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Proposed initial tools |

| 42 | Should the agent proactively contact a rejected applicant or only respond when the applicant asks for help? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether the agent should proactively reach out to rejected applicants or only respond on request. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 43 | Should the applicant have to authenticate before speaking to the agent? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design requires the applicant to authenticate (via a session token) before interacting with the agent; in production this will be replaced by real authentication such as OTP or lender SSO. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/server.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. ## 3. APPLICANT DATA / PROFILE

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 57 | What information about the applicant is actually available to the lender? | ANSWERED | The lender can see the applicant’s Customer ID and name (required), optionally email and phone, and the custom underwriting fields mapped from Frappe such as monthly income, monthly obligations, credit score, employment months, loan amount and tenure; address, employee path and sensitive data like PAN are not provided. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/frappe_adapter.py` |
| 58 | What information about the applicant is actually available to our agent? | ANSWERED | The agent only sees the applicant’s identifier and name (and later email/phone), plus the application‑level fields used by ZEN (income, obligations, credit score, employment months) and any loan history for context. It does not have access to address, employee‑path data, PAN, OTP secrets or other sensitive fields. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § A. Applicant identity/reference; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records |
| 59 | What information is available at application time? | ANSWERED | At application time the system records the application ID, applicant ID, monthly income, monthly obligations, credit score, employment months, requested loan amount, loan tenure (repayment periods) and the application status. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/frappe_adapter.py`; `docs/prototypes/p0-data-mapping.md` § P0 Data Mapping |
| 60 | What information becomes available only after underwriting? | PARTIAL | After underwriting, fields such as the decision reason, reason code, notes, rule‑level results, score and a link to the decision evidence become available (stored in custom fields). | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 10. Decision storage; `infra/frappe/bootstrap/seed_site.py` |
| 61 | What information comes from the applicant? | ANSWERED | The applicant provides their Customer ID and name (required), optionally email and phone, and the custom application fields such as monthly income, monthly obligations, credit score, employment months, loan amount and tenure; sensitive data like PAN or OTP are not used. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/prototypes/p0-data-mapping.md` § P0 Data Mapping; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § A. Applicant identity/reference |
| 62 | What information comes from external systems? | ANSWERED | The external Frappe Lending system supplies the applicant’s core financial fields – monthly income, monthly obligations, credit score, employment months – as well as the loan amount, tenure and identifiers (application_id, applicant_id, external_ref). | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/runtime.py` |
| 63 | What information comes from credit bureaus? | ANSWERED | The credit bureau supplies the applicant’s consumer credit information, specifically the credit score (bureau score) from TransUnion CIBIL. | policy source BFL-APP-TNC-2023 (approved) <https://cms-assets.bajajfinserv.in/is/content/bajajfinance/general-loan-application-terms-clean-8-june-23pdf>; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/facts.py` |
| 64 | What information comes from the underwriting system? | ANSWERED | The underwriting system (ZEN) supplies decision evidence that includes rule IDs, names, versions, input references, pass/fail outcomes, reason codes, threshold sources, execution timestamps, and trace references, as well as the numeric context (income, obligations, credit score, employment months). | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `decision_engine/schemas/underwriting.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 2 --- data pipeline, conventional automation and decision engine |
| 65 | What information comes from the lender's CRM? | ANSWERED | The lender’s CRM (Frappe Lending) supplies the applicant’s core identity (customer ID and name) plus optional contact details (email, phone) and, from the loan application, the custom fields for monthly income, monthly obligations, credit score, employment months, loan amount and tenure. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/frappe_adapter.py`; `docs/prototypes/p0-data-mapping.md` § P0 Data Mapping |
| 66 | What information comes from previous applications? | ANSWERED | The system can pull previous and current loans for each applicant, including each loan’s status, totals, NPA flag, repayment rows with days past due, and schedule rows. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 11. Historical data |
| 67 | What information comes from repayment history? | ANSWERED | The repayment history supplies fields such as days past due, NPA flag, repayment type, amount paid, principal and interest paid, penalty amount and schedule details. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 6. Repayment history |
| 68 | What applicant attributes are relevant to explaining a rejection? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 69 | What applicant attributes are relevant to determining remediation steps? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 70 | What applicant attributes are relevant only to communication? | ANSWERED | The project treats the applicant’s email address and phone number (and the name for display) as optional fields used only for communication, not for underwriting. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § A. Applicant identity/reference |
| 71 | What applicant attributes are irrelevant and should not be collected? | ANSWERED | The project treats address fields, employee‑path data, PAN numbers and OTP secrets as irrelevant and should not be collected. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 3. Loan Lead (`loan_origination/doctype/loan_lead/`) |
| 72 | Is locality relevant to the problem? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether applicant locality is a factor in the underwriting or advisory process; only synthetic personas include a city tier, but no rule or mapping uses it. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 73 | Is educational qualification relevant to the problem? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether an applicant's educational qualification is relevant to the loan decision; the team must clarify. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 74 | Is occupation relevant to the problem? | PARTIAL | Regulatory guidance and the reference lender’s public material state that occupation is a required part of the borrower’s economic profile, but the prototype’s synthetic decision model does not use occupation as an input (it uses months in current job instead). | policy source RBI-DLD-2025 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12848&Mode=0>; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); policy source BFL-PL-RATES-FEES (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan-processing-fees-and-interest-rates> |
| 75 | Is industry relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Yes. Industry segment is listed as a factor in lender policies for calculating loan spreads, showing it is relevant to loan decisions. | policy source BFL-IRP-2023 (approved_historical) <https://cms-assets.bajajfinserv.in/is/content/bajajfinance/interest-rate-policy-v1pdf?fmt=pdf&scl=1> |
| 76 | Is employment tenure relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Yes – employment tenure is a required factor; the policy enforces a minimum of 12 months of continuous employment. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule MIN_EMPLOYMENT_TENURE (reason code ERR_EMPLOYMENT_TENURE_SHORT); policy source SYN-POLICY-V1 (approved) <repo://data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md>; `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py` |
| 77 | Is employment type relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | No – employment type is not used in the current underwriting problem; the decision model only checks employment tenure, not salaried vs self‑employed. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 3. Loan Lead (`loan_origination/doctype/loan_lead/`); `docs/prototypes/p0-data-mapping.md` § P0 Data Mapping |
| 78 | Is income relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Yes. Income is a core factor in the underwriting model, used in the MIN_MONTHLY_INCOME rule and the FOIR calculation. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule MIN_MONTHLY_INCOME (reason code ERR_INCOME_BELOW_MINIMUM); `decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json`; `docs/prototypes/p0-data-mapping.md` § P0 Data Mapping; `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule FOIR_MAX_45 (reason code ERR_FOIR_EXCEEDED) |
| 79 | Is income stability relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Yes. Income stability is a key factor for personal‑loan eligibility and is explicitly considered in the underwriting model (e.g., via employment months). | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; policy source BFL-INSIGHT-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/insights/4-reasons-for-personal-loan-rejection-and-how-to-avoid-them> |
| 80 | Is existing debt relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Yes – existing debt is a key factor in the underwriting model; the system captures a debt‑to‑income (or FOIR) ratio and the synthetic policy rejects applicants whose debt ratio exceeds the threshold. | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; `scripts/data/audit_datasets.py` |
| 81 | Is credit history relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Yes – credit history, captured via the applicant’s credit score, is a core factor in the underwriting model and thus essential to the advisory problem. | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; `docs/prototypes/p0-data-mapping.md` § P0 Data Mapping |
| 82 | Is previous loan history relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Previous loan history is not used in the core underwriting decision (ZEN input), but it will be used later by the advisory agent for explanations and re‑application guidance. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 11. Historical data; `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 20. Final table |
| 83 | Are previous applications relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Previous applications (historical data) are not used in the ZEN underwriting input; they are only used by the agent for explanation and re‑application guidance. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 11. Historical data |
| 84 | Is repayment behavior relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Repayment behavior is not used in the underwriting decision (ZEN input) but is kept as historical context for the advisory agent to explain rejections and guide re‑applications. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 11. Historical data |
| 85 | Is language preference relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system treats language preference as a variable that determines the language of the agent’s explanation (en/hi/te), but it does not influence the underwriting rules themselves. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 86 | Is the applicant's interaction history relevant to the problem? | ANSWERED | Interaction history is not used in the ZEN underwriting decision (the policy uses only application data), but it is kept as context for the advisory agent to explain decisions and guide re‑applications. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 11. Historical data |
| 87 | Which data is necessary for the system to function? | ANSWERED | The system only needs the applicant’s identifier – the Customer record ID and name – to function; all other fields are optional or not required for the prototype. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records |
| 88 | Which data is optional? | ANSWERED | The optional applicant data are the contact fields – email and phone (and related name fields) – while address and employee‑path data are not needed for the prototype. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § A. Applicant identity/reference |
| 89 | Which data is potentially sensitive? | ANSWERED | The potentially sensitive data are the applicant’s PAN (only on the Loan Lead) and OTP secrets, which the system never accesses. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 3. Loan Lead (`loan_origination/doctype/loan_lead/`) |
| 90 | Which data should never be used for communication adaptation? | ANSWERED | Only the answer language, the applicant‑chosen literacy level, and the applicant’s own recorded facts may be used to adapt communication; all other data – especially protected attributes and sensitive personal data such as PAN or OTP secrets – must never be used for communication adaptation. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41); `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records |
| 91 | Which data should never influence the explanation itself? | ANSWERED | The explanation must not be influenced by any sensitive or irrelevant data – e.g., PAN, OTP secrets, other applicants’ information, internal grades/scores, address or employee‑path fields – and should only use the applicant’s own submitted values and the rule checks. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records |
| 92 | Which data should never be used to infer applicant intelligence, personality, socioeconomic status, or similar characteristics? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 93 | What is the minimum applicant profile required to provide a useful explanation? | ANSWERED | The minimal profile needed is just the applicant’s Customer ID and name; other data (email, phone) are optional and address, employee path, PAN, etc. are not required for explanations. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records |
| 94 | What is the minimum applicant profile required to provide a remediation plan? | ANSWERED | The system only needs the applicant’s identifier and name – the Customer id + name – to generate a remediation plan; all other fields are optional or not needed. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § A. Applicant identity/reference |
| 95 | What is the minimum applicant profile required to answer a "what-if" question? | PARTIAL | The project defines the minimal applicant profile as a Customer record with just the customer ID and name (other fields like email/phone are optional). | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records |
| 96 | What information should the agent request rather than infer? | PARTIAL | The agent should explicitly request applicant‑provided data (e.g., income, monthly obligations, credit score, employment tenure and other required/optional fields) rather than infer them; it must not infer policy thresholds or edit recorded information. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 2 --- data pipeline, conventional automation and decision engine |
| 97 | What information should the agent never infer? | ANSWERED | The agent must never infer the actual lender policy from correlations in the data and present it as a source‑backed rule. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Data limitations |

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 98 | Should the system maintain a communication profile for the applicant? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the system should maintain a communication profile for the applicant; this decision is not documented. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 99 | What exactly should a communication profile represent? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not define what a communication profile should represent; the team must provide that definition. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 100 | Should the system estimate the applicant's financial knowledge? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the system should estimate the applicant's financial knowledge; this design decision is not addressed. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 101 | How should financial knowledge be defined? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not define financial knowledge; the project team must supply that definition. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 102 | What dimensions of financial knowledge should be measured? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify which dimensions of financial knowledge should be measured; the project team or VaaniLabs must define them. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 103 | Should the system distinguish between basic, intermediate, and advanced understanding? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system includes an applicant‑chosen literacy level (via the `Literacy` enum in contracts.py) to tailor explanations for basic, intermediate and advanced understanding. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 104 | How can the system detect that an applicant understands a financial concept? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify a method for detecting an applicant's understanding of a financial concept; this would require additional design or user research. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 105 | How can the system detect that an applicant does not understand a financial concept? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 106 | What conversational signals indicate the applicant's level of understanding? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 107 | Should the system ask explicit questions to assess understanding? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not indicate whether the system should ask explicit questions to assess applicant understanding. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 108 | Can the system infer knowledge from the applicant's questions? | ANSWERED | No. The system does not infer new knowledge from an applicant’s questions; it only uses the question to retrieve policy passages and generate explanations, never as a shadow underwriting engine. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 109 | Can the system infer knowledge from the applicant's responses? | ANSWERED | No – the system does not infer new knowledge from applicant responses; it only orchestrates evidence and policy retrieval to generate grounded explanations. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Target later architecture |
| 110 | Can the system infer knowledge from the applicant's use of financial terminology? | ANSWERED | No – the system does not infer knowledge from the applicant’s terminology; it relies on an explicit literacy level supplied in the request and uses predefined response styles. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 111 | How reliable would such inference be? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 112 | How should the system handle uncertainty about the applicant's knowledge? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how the system should act when it is uncertain about the applicant's knowledge level. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 113 | Should the applicant be allowed to select their preferred explanation level? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system is designed to let each applicant choose their preferred explanation level (low, medium, or high literacy) via the applicant‑chosen literacy setting. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 114 | Should the applicant be allowed to choose simple, standard, or detailed explanations? | N/A | The system does not provide a selectable option for simple, standard, or detailed explanations; instead it automatically selects the explanation style (low, medium, high) based on the applicant’s literacy profile. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 115 | Should the applicant be allowed to change this preference at any time? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the applicant may change the document‑collection preference at any time; only that a preference can be given. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 116 | Should the applicant's preferred language be explicit? | ANSWERED | Yes – the applicant’s preferred language must be captured explicitly, as RBI guidance requires communication in the borrower‑chosen language and the system uses that field to set the response language. | policy source RBI-RBC-NBFC-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/362MD26CA543937BA439A97E1BCFC08CF5808.PDF>; `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 117 | Should Telugu be supported? | ANSWERED | Yes, Telugu should be supported; the project includes Telugu in its language map and tests confirm Telugu locale is handled, which aligns with RBI guidance to communicate in the borrower’s chosen language. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `tests/test_agent_graph.py`; policy source RBI-RBC-NBFC-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/362MD26CA543937BA439A97E1BCFC08CF5808.PDF> |
| 118 | Should language preference be permanent or session-based? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether language preference should be stored permanently or only for a session; the decision must be provided by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 119 | Should explanation complexity change dynamically during the conversation? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design includes dynamic adaptation of explanation complexity to the applicant’s loan‑literacy level, with a dedicated communication‑adaptation metric to evaluate readability differences. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); memory: project note (loan-advisory-agent) § Loan Advisory Agent (VaaniLabs.AI capstone; repo Narendarcodes/Load-Advisory-Agent, "Load" spelling) |
| 120 | What evidence should cause the system to change explanation complexity? | PARTIAL | The system should change explanation complexity when evidence about the applicant’s financial literacy (their knowledge profile) indicates a low, medium, or high level, selecting the corresponding LITERACY_STYLE template. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 121 | How quickly should the system adapt? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how quickly the system should adapt; this requires input from the lender or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 122 | Should the system remember that the applicant already understands a concept? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the system should retain that an applicant already understands a concept; this decision must be provided by the project designers. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 123 | Should the agent avoid explaining concepts the applicant has already demonstrated understanding of? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether the agent should avoid explaining concepts the applicant has already shown understanding of; this decision must be provided by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 124 | How should the agent behave when the applicant repeatedly misunderstands the same concept? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify a distinct behavior for when an applicant repeatedly misunderstands the same concept; the team must define this policy. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 125 | How should the agent verify that the applicant understood the explanation? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify a method for the agent to verify that the applicant understood the explanation; this decision must be provided by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 126 | Should the agent use examples? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether the agent should use examples in its explanations; clarification is needed from the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 127 | Should the agent use numerical examples based on the applicant's own data? | ANSWERED | Yes – the agent must only use the applicant’s own numbers when giving examples, never inventing or adding other figures. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 128 | Should the agent use analogies? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether the agent should use analogies; this design decision must be answered by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 129 | How can we prevent analogies from introducing incorrect financial interpretations? | PARTIAL | The system uses guard patterns, strict evidence‑only responses and evaluation metrics (e.g., hallucination detection) to limit unsupported claims, which helps curb analogies that could mislead, but no explicit rule on analogies is provided. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py` |
| 130 | Should the agent change response length based on the applicant's behavior? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether the agent should change response length based on applicant behavior. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 131 | Should the agent change technical vocabulary based on the applicant's behavior? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design permits the agent to adjust its wording (including technical vocabulary) to match the applicant’s chosen literacy level, not arbitrarily based on other behavior. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 132 | Should the agent change tone based on the applicant's behavior? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the agent should change tone based on applicant behavior; no rule or decision on tone adaptation is provided. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 133 | What communication adaptations are useful? | ANSWERED | The system adapts communication by adjusting literacy‑based style, selecting the borrower’s regional language (or chosen language), and using a spoken voice channel for audio delivery; for illiterate borrowers RBI guidance also recommends explaining terms in the presence of a non‑employee witness. | `tests/test_agent_graph.py`; policy source RBI-RBC-NBFC-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/362MD26CA543937BA439A97E1BCFC08CF5808.PDF>; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 134 | Which communication adaptations could become patronizing or inappropriate? | UNKNOWN | The project materials do not specify which communication adaptations might be patronizing or inappropriate; the team or VaaniLabs must define these guidelines. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 135 | How do we ensure that adaptation does not become stereotyping? | UNKNOWN | The project mitigates stereotyping by measuring adaptation through the A2 evaluation metric, which compares low‑vs‑high literacy explanations for the same persona, but it does not test protected attributes or provide broader safeguards. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 136 | Should the communication profile persist across sessions? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the communication profile should persist across sessions; this decision must be provided by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 137 | If it persists, for how long? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** a partner lender |  |
| 138 | Should the applicant be able to inspect the communication profile? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the applicant can inspect the communication profile; the project team or VaaniLabs must decide this. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 139 | Should the applicant be able to correct or override the profile? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 140 | Could an incorrect communication profile harm the applicant? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not discuss the impact of an incorrect communication (knowledge) profile on the applicant, so it cannot be determined from the provided documentation. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 141 | How do we measure whether adaptation actually improves comprehension? | UNKNOWN | The project measures adaptation via the A2 literacy‑pair metric, which compares low‑vs‑high literacy answer versions by computing readability/jargon differences and a judge’s rating of the paired responses. This is the only built‑in gauge, though it does not directly test applicant comprehension. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 142 | How do we distinguish "simple language" from "oversimplification"? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 143 | How do we ensure that simplifying an explanation does not remove important caveats? | ANSWERED | We enforce that simplifications retain all caveats by measuring explanation completeness – the reason_value_coverage metric checks that every failed ZEN reason and its value appear in the explanation – and by using deterministic normalisation and guard templates that preserve all decision evidence when generating simplified text. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44) |

| 144 | What does a real personal-loan application record contain? | ANSWERED | A real personal‑loan application record includes the applicant link (Customer), loan product, loan amount, repayment periods, status, posting date, and four custom underwriting fields (monthly income, obligations, credit score, employment months). | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 4–5. Required DocTypes / fields; `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 20. Final table |
