Field mapping (Frappe → canonical → ZEN) |
| 148 | Which values are verified externally? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify which applicant values are verified by external sources; this information must be supplied by the lender or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 149 | Which values can change after application? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 150 | Which values are used directly by underwriting? | ANSWERED | The underwriting engine uses the applicant’s monthly income, monthly obligations, credit score, employment months, loan amount, and loan tenure months directly. | `decision_engine/schemas/underwriting.py` |
| 151 | Which values are used only for verification? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify which loan‑application values are used only for verification; this must be answered by the project team or the lender. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 152 | Which values are used only for customer communication? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify which values are used only for customer communication; the project documentation and policy excerpts do not list communication‑only fields. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 153 | What loan products are within scope? | ANSWERED | The project scope is limited to personal loan products (the synthetic personal loan used in the prototype). | `config/lender_profiles/generic_nbfc.yaml`; `config/lender_profiles/generic_bank.yaml`; `docs/prototypes/p0-frappe.md` § Relevant DocTypes (Lending v16); `infra/frappe/bootstrap/seed_site.py` |
| 154 | Is the system limited to personal loans? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system is designed only for personal‑loan products, as all lender profiles and the synthetic policy list ‘personal_loan’ as the sole product. | `config/lender_profiles/synthetic_prototype.yaml`; `config/lender_profiles/generic_nbfc.yaml`; `config/lender_profiles/generic_bank.yaml`; `tests/test_p3_workflow.py` |
| 155 | What happens if the applicant asks about another type of loan? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how the system should respond when an applicant asks about a loan type other than personal loans; clarification from the project team is needed. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 156 | Should the agent support multiple loan products? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the agent should support multiple loan products; they focus on a single synthetic personal loan product. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 157 | If multiple products are supported, can their policies differ? | PARTIAL | The design stores underwriting rules only in ZEN decision models, not in the loan‑product definition, so different products could be linked to different policies, but the materials do not explicitly state that multiple products will have distinct policies. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 4. Loan Product (`loan_management/doctype/loan_product/`); `README.md` § Where things live |
| 158 | How are product-specific policies represented? | ANSWERED | Product‑specific policies are represented as JSON policy files in the decision engine (e.g., decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json), which the ZEN engine consumes. | `README.md` § Where things live |
| 159 | Can the same applicant be eligible for one product and not another? | PARTIAL | The project notes that loan products do not contain eligibility rules; they only provide context such as limits and rates, while eligibility logic lives in the ZEN engine, so eligibility could vary by product but the materials do not state a definitive answer. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 4. Loan Product (`loan_management/doctype/loan_product/`) |
| 160 | Should the agent explain alternative products? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify whether the agent should explain alternative loan products. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 161 | If yes, what evidence and policy would it need? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system requires a Decision Evidence record (including rule‑level outcomes, decision, and policy metadata) produced by the ZEN engine, and the corresponding policy JSON (personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json) that defines the rules. | `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py`; `tests/test_p3_workflow.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/evidence_store.py` |

## 6. DECISION ENGINE

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 162 | What exactly is the lender's decision engine? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not describe the lender’s actual decision engine; they only detail a synthetic ZEN Engine used for the prototype. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 163 | Is the decision engine rule-based, model-based, hybrid, or unknown? | ANSWERED | The decision engine is rule‑based. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § P0 --- decision core |
| 164 | What does the decision engine return? | ANSWERED | The decision engine returns a structured response containing a result object with the final decision (e.g., APPROVED or REJECTED), per‑rule pass/fail flags and values, plus an execution trace and performance metrics. | `tests/test_zen_smoke.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py` |
| 165 | Does it return only approve/reject? | ANSWERED | No. The ZEN decision engine returns a structured result that includes the decision (APPROVED or REJECTED) plus additional fields such as rule pass flags and FOIR values. | `docs/prototypes/p0-zen.md` § Python API (verified on this machine); `tests/test_zen_smoke.py` |
| 166 | Does it return structured reason codes? | ANSWERED | Yes. The decision engine includes structured reason codes in its evidence record – each rule evaluation carries a `reason_code` and a list of `failed_reason_codes` is generated for rejected applications. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`); `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 10. Decision storage |
| 167 | Does it return multiple reason codes? | ANSWERED | Yes. The decision engine aggregates all failed rule reason codes into a list (failed_reason_codes), so a single decision can include multiple reason codes. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`) |
| 168 | Does it rank reason codes? | ANSWERED | The system does not rank reason codes; it simply records the failed reason codes in the order they appear. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/facts.py` |
| 169 | Does it return feature values? | ANSWERED | Yes. The decision engine’s evidence record includes the input feature values (the four scored fields) under the `inputs.values` field. | `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`); `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 170 | Does it return thresholds? | ANSWERED | Yes. The decision engine includes each rule's threshold in the returned evidence (e.g., the JSON evaluation contains a "threshold" field) and the UI displays it in the decision card. | `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py`; `apps/web/chat/index.html` |
| 171 | Does it return model explanations? | ANSWERED | No, the system does not return model explanations; it provides structured decision evidence (failed rule IDs and reasons) rather than post‑hoc model interpretability. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § ZEN Engine behavior discovered |
| 172 | Does it return confidence or probability? | ANSWERED | The decision engine returns a deterministic decision (APPROVED/REJECTED) and per‑rule PASS/FAIL outcomes (plus a numeric FOIR), but no confidence score or probability. | `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py`; `tests/test_zen_smoke.py` |
| 173 | Does it return a risk score? | ANSWERED | No, the decision engine does not return a risk score; it returns a decision (APPROVED/REJECTED), rule pass/fail results and related metrics like FOIR, but no risk score field. | `tests/test_zen_smoke.py`; `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py` |
| 174 | Does it return a policy rule that failed? | ANSWERED | Yes – the decision engine includes the failed policy rule(s) in its decision evidence, returning them as rejection reasons. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 175 | Can the agent access the decision engine directly? | ANSWERED | No. The agent does not access the ZEN decision engine directly; it only consumes the Decision Evidence produced by the engine. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Target later architecture |
| 176 | Should the agent access the decision engine directly? | ANSWERED | No. The agent must not call the decision engine directly; it only consumes the Decision Evidence produced by ZEN via the orchestrator. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Target later architecture |
| 177 | Should the agent instead consume a decision record? | ANSWERED | No – the design specifies the agent should consume Decision Evidence, not a decision record. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 178 | What is the minimum decision information required to explain a rejection? | ANSWERED | The minimum information needed is the set of failed rule evaluations recorded in the Decision Evidence – i.e. each rule’s ID, its reason code, the actual applicant value, the threshold and the FAIL result. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py` |
| 179 | Can the reason for rejection be reconstructed if the decision engine does not provide reason codes? | PARTIAL | The system records rejection reasons as the failed rules with their reason codes in the Decision Evidence record; if the engine omitted reason codes, the recorded evidence would lack them, so reconstruction would not be possible as designed. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`) |
| 180 | Can an explanation be generated from only the final decision? | ANSWERED | No – the system requires Decision Evidence (rule outcomes) to generate an explanation; the final decision alone is insufficient. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § ZEN Engine behavior discovered; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 181 | What happens if the decision engine provides insufficient explanation data? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify what the system does when the decision engine yields insufficient explanation data; clarification from the project team is required. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 182 | What happens if the decision engine returns contradictory information? | ANSWERED | If the decision engine returns contradictory information (or an engine error), the application is routed to the MANUAL_REVIEW state for human review. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 183 | What happens if the decision changes after the applicant has started a conversation? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 184 | What happens if the application is under manual review? | ANSWERED | When an application reaches the MANUAL_REVIEW state, the system hands it off to a human reviewer; the agent replies with a handoff message saying a human will decide, and the reviewer can then approve, reject, or request more information. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Lifecycle (prototype workflow, not a regulatory requirement); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 185 | What happens if the decision is pending? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what the system does when a decision is pending; this behavior is not documented. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 186 | What happens if the decision cannot be retrieved? | ANSWERED | If the decision cannot be retrieved, the orchestrator returns an ERROR evidence object and never fabricates a decision. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py` |
| 187 | What happens if the decision record is corrupted or incomplete? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not describe the system's behavior when a decision record is corrupted or incomplete. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |

## 7. REJECTION REASONS

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 188 | What are the common reasons for personal-loan rejection? | ANSWERED | Common reasons include low credit score, irregular or insufficient income, high existing debt or financial commitments, inaccurate or incomplete application details, and non‑compliance with lender‑specific eligibility policies. | policy source BFL-INSIGHT-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/insights/4-reasons-for-personal-loan-rejection-and-how-to-avoid-them> |
| 189 | How are rejection reasons represented in real lending systems? | PARTIAL | In real lending systems, banks are required to convey the main rejection reason(s) in writing, while NBFCs typically notify applicants via SMS and may not provide coded reasons. | policy source RBI-FPC-2007 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_CircularIndexDisplay.aspx?Id=3315>; `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection |
| 190 | Are rejection reasons standardized? | ANSWERED | No – rejection reasons are not standardized; they vary by lender type (banks must give written reasons, NBFCs have no such requirement and may not assign a reason). | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection; run report: out/loan-bajaj/RESULTS.md § 1. Source register v2: 38 sources (verified 2026-10-14) |
| 191 | Are reason codes standardized across lenders? | UNKNOWN | The project materials do not state whether reason codes are standardized across lenders; this can only be answered by a partner lender. **Who must answer:** a partner lender |  |
| 192 | Can one rejection have multiple reasons? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system records each rule that fails, so a single rejection can include multiple recorded reasons. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § 1. Project objective and product definition |
| 193 | Which reason is the "main reason"? | PARTIAL | The system records all failed underwriting rules as rejection reasons and does not single out a "main" reason; RBI policy requires banks to convey the main reason(s) in writing, but NBFCs like Bajaj have no such clause. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); run report: out/loan-bajaj/RESULTS.md § 1. Source register v2: 38 sources (verified 2026-10-14) |
| 194 | Can several reasons independently cause rejection? | ANSWERED | Yes. The ZEN engine records every rule that fails, and any failed rule triggers a rejection, so multiple independent reasons can each cause a rejection. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 195 | Are some reasons more actionable than others? | ANSWERED | Yes. The project’s source material notes that some rejection reasons, such as a low credit score, are considered more actionable because they can be improved, whereas other reasons (e.g., internal policy constraints) are less so. | policy source BFL-INSIGHT-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/insights/4-reasons-for-personal-loan-rejection-and-how-to-avoid-them> |
| 196 | Are some rejection reasons not customer-shareable? | ANSWERED | Yes. The project treats internal scoring‑model details and other confidential information as non‑shareable, and the guard `not_available` blocks exposing them to customers. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection |
| 197 | Which reasons can legally or operationally be disclosed? | ANSWERED | Banks must disclose the main reason(s) for rejection in writing per RBI RBC‑CB para 331, while NBFCs have no RBI‑mandated written‑reason clause and may disclose only at their discretion; internal scoring models are treated as confidential unless the lender explicitly permits disclosure. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection; run report: out/loan-bajaj/RESULTS.md § 1. Source register v2: 38 sources (verified 2026-10-14) |
| 198 | Which reasons must be explained in writing? | ANSWERED | Banks must convey the main reason(s) for a loan rejection in writing, while NBFCs have no RBI clause requiring written reasons. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection |
| 199 | What happens when no clear reason exists? | PARTIAL | When ZEN does not produce any failed rule (i.e., there is no clear rejection reason), the agent is not permitted to invent a reason; it will fall back to a generic template or abstain, because it may only state the recorded failed rules as reasons. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 200 | What happens when the reason is "high risk"? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what the system does when a rejection reason is "high risk"; only that lenders may categorize customers as high risk, but no behavior is described. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 201 | Can the agent translate "high risk" into more specific information? | ANSWERED | No – the agent cannot break down a generic “high risk” label into finer details; it must reply that internal risk‑grade information isn’t available to it. | `tests/test_abstain_unavailable.py` |
| 202 | Under what conditions would such a translation be valid? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify the conditions under which a translation of rejection reasons would be valid; this requires input from the lender or VaaniLabs to define policy translation rules. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 203 | What happens when the reason is a model-derived signal rather than a policy rule? | N/A | The system only records and explains rule failures; model‑derived signals are not used as rejection reasons. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 204 | How should model-driven reasons be communicated? | ANSWERED | Model‑driven reasons must be conveyed in writing for banks (RBI‑RBC‑CB para 331) but for NBFCs only an acknowledgement is required; the agent must therefore tailor communication to the lender type and avoid over‑claiming written reasons for NBFCs. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection |
| 205 | How should rule-driven reasons be communicated? | ANSWERED | Rule‑driven reasons must be communicated by directly presenting the recorded failed rules from the decision evidence, without adding any ungrounded details or claims about lender‑specific written‑reason obligations. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection; `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 206 | How should multiple reasons be prioritized in the explanation? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how to prioritize multiple rejection reasons in the explanation; this design decision must be provided by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 207 | Should all rejection reasons be shown? | ANSWERED | No – only banks are required to provide written reasons; NBFCs like Bajaj have no such obligation, and the agent is designed not to claim that all reasons must be shown. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection; run report: out/loan-bajaj/RESULTS.md § 1. Source register v2: 38 sources (verified 2026-10-14) |
| 208 | Should some reasons be grouped? | ANSWERED | The prototype records each failed rule as a separate rejection reason and does not group reasons together. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 209 | Can the agent ever infer an additional reason that was not recorded? | ANSWERED | No. The agent is designed to only state the recorded failed rules and never invent additional rejection reasons. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries |
| 210 | What happens when the applicant believes the recorded reason is incorrect? | ANSWERED | The agent does not edit the record; it creates a data‑dispute handoff to a human reviewer and informs the applicant that a reviewer will check the recorded information. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 211 | What happens when applicant-provided information conflicts with the recorded reason? | ANSWERED | The agent does not edit the application; it escalates the conflict to a human reviewer via a data‑dispute handoff and informs the applicant it cannot change the record. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |

## 8. EVIDENCE

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 212 | What counts as evidence for a rejection explanation? | ANSWERED | Evidence for a rejection explanation is the Decision Evidence record, which contains the failed rule evaluations, their reason codes, actual values, thresholds, policy version and trace, combined with the relevant policy. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py` |
| 213 | What source should be considered authoritative for each applicant attribute? | PARTIAL | The project designates official RBI directives and the lender’s publicly‑available eligibility documents (e.g., HDFC‑PL‑ELIG) as the authoritative sources for applicant attributes, as recorded in the policy register and source‑priority list. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 3 --- policy document collection and governance; `data/sources/policy_register.yaml` |
| 214 | How does the system know which value was actually used in the decision? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 215 | Can applicant data and decision-engine data differ? | ANSWERED | Yes. Applicant data may differ because it is explicitly mapped and validated into a canonical schema before reaching the ZEN decision engine; raw Frappe data never enters ZEN unchanged. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 216 | What happens when two systems contain different values? | ANSWERED | When two sources contain different values the system does not arbitrarily choose one; it retains both values and surfaces the conflict (e.g., the LB‑024 test surfaced the conflict instead of picking a side). | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources) |
| 217 | How should data conflicts be resolved? | ANSWERED | Data conflicts are resolved by retaining all conflicting entries, surfacing the conflict to the user, and citing the source page and date without arbitrarily choosing one version. | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources) |
| 218 | Should every explanation claim have an evidence reference? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state a rule that every explanation claim must include an evidence reference; clarification from the project team or VaaniLabs is needed. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 219 | Should the applicant be shown the evidence supporting each claim? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design explicitly provides the applicant with the decision evidence (rule evaluations marked customer_visible) when explaining a rejection or eligibility question. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |
| 220 | How much evidence should be disclosed? | PARTIAL | The project records a full Decision Evidence record with rule‑level outcomes, each flagged by a `customer_visible` field; evidence disclosed to the applicant should include those rule evaluations marked visible, but the materials do not state a complete policy on the exact amount to disclose. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`) |
| 221 | Can the applicant see the exact values used by the lender? | ANSWERED | No – the applicant only sees their own decision, the rule checks and the values they submitted; lender‑specific values such as internal grades or thresholds are not shown. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 222 | Can the applicant see where each value came from? | PARTIAL | The system’s decision evidence records include each rule’s field name and the applicant’s actual value, and the explanation prompt instructs the agent to show the applicant’s value and the required threshold, so applicants see their own values but not the deeper provenance of those values. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 223 | Can the applicant challenge a particular value? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system includes a deterministic “dispute” guard that lets an applicant challenge a specific value before the LLM agent runs. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`) |
| 224 | What happens if the evidence is missing? | ANSWERED | If evidence is missing the system does not fabricate a decision; it returns an ERROR evidence record, flags the issue and routes the case for clarification or human escalation (e.g., INFO_REQUESTED). | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 225 | What happens if the evidence is stale? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what the system does when evidence becomes stale; this requires clarification from the project team or lender. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 226 | What happens if the evidence is inconsistent? | ANSWERED | If the evidence is inconsistent, the system treats it as a failure and returns an ERROR evidence object, routing the application to manual review rather than fabricating a decision. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 227 | What happens if the evidence is not customer-shareable? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what the system does when evidence is marked as not customer‑shareable; clarification from the project team is required. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 228 | How should the agent communicate uncertainty in evidence? | PARTIAL | The project states that the agent should surface the synthetic nature of the policy and thresholds as a caveat in its explanations, labeling them as team assumptions rather than real‑lender rules. This provides a form of uncertainty communication, but no broader guidance on handling other evidence uncertainty is given. | PR #6 (feat/langgraph-agent): Phase 5: single LangGraph advisory agent over ZEN evidence, Frappe and policy RAG (explains, never decides) § Please read: honest caveats; `docs/agent/README.md` § Phase 5 — Loan Advisory Agent (one LangGraph graph) + Phase 6 voice |
| 229 | How can we test whether an explanation is faithful to the underlying evidence? | ANSWERED | We test faithfulness by automatically checking that the explanation reproduces the ZEN decision evidence (e.g., using the `reason_value_coverage` metric to ensure every failed reason appears) and by verifying citation faithfulness with the `citation_faithful` metric in the evaluation harness. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 3. Published metric families for the evaluation questions (s.44, s.45); `scripts/agent/eval_agent.py` |
| 230 | How can we detect hallucinated evidence? | PARTIAL | The project suggests detecting hallucinated evidence by applying reference‑free metrics such as FactScore, which breaks a generation into atomic facts and measures the percentage supported by a reliable knowledge source. This is listed among the published metric families for evaluation. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 3. Published metric families for the evaluation questions (s.44, s.45) |

## 9. LENDER POLICY

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 231 | What exactly is the lender eligibility policy? | PARTIAL | The lender eligibility policy is drawn from each lender’s public eligibility page and includes criteria such as Indian nationality, age between 21‑80 years, a minimum CIBIL score of 650, and employment with a public, private or MNC (or being salaried/self‑employed), with additional income and experience thresholds where specified. | policy source BFL-PL-ELIG-DOCS (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan-eligibility-and-documents>; policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility> |
| 232 | Where does the policy come from? | ANSWERED | The policy comes from the team‑authored synthetic prototype file `decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json` (registered as a synthetic policy in the policy registry, not a real lender policy). | `README.md` § Where things live; `data/sources/policy_register.yaml` |
| 233 | What format is the policy provided in? | ANSWERED | The policy is supplied as a JSON file (e.g., personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json). | `README.md` § Where things live; `decision_engine/policies/registry.json` |
| 234 | Is the policy a PDF, document, database, rules engine, API, or something else? | ANSWERED | The policy is a synthetic JSON file stored in the repository (decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json) and loaded by the ZEN engine via the policy registry, not a PDF, database, or API. | `README.md` § Where things live; `decision_engine/policies/registry.py` |
| 235 | How are policy rules represented? | ANSWERED | Policy rules are defined as JSON objects in the ZEN decision model files (e.g., `decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json`). Each rule records its ID, name, version, reason code, target field, operator, threshold, source, description and visibility, and these attributes are emitted into the decision‑evidence record. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 236 | Which policy rules are relevant to personal-loan eligibility? | ANSWERED | The relevant policy rules are the eligibility criteria published by lenders: Indian nationality, age limits (21‑60 for banks, 21‑80 for Bajaj), minimum CIBIL score (650‑720), employment type (salaried or self‑employed in public, private or MNC), minimum monthly income (e.g., ₹25,000), and required identity and income documents. | policy source BFL-PL-OVERVIEW (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan>; policy source BFL-PL-ELIG-DOCS (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan-eligibility-and-documents>; policy source HDFC-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.hdfcbank.com/personal/borrow/popular-loans/personal-loan/eligibility> |
| 237 | Which policy rules concern income? | ANSWERED | The policy defines two income‑related rules: MIN_MONTHLY_INCOME (minimum monthly income) and FOIR_MAX_45 (maximum fixed‑obligation‑to‑income ratio). | `decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json`; `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule MIN_MONTHLY_INCOME (reason code ERR_INCOME_BELOW_MINIMUM) |
| 238 | Which concern credit score? | ANSWERED | The policy’s MIN_CREDIT_SCORE rule governs credit‑score requirements, requiring a score of at least 700. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule MIN_CREDIT_SCORE (reason code ERR_CREDIT_SCORE_BELOW_MINIMUM); policy source SYN-POLICY-V1 (approved) <repo://data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md>; `decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json`; `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py` |
| 239 | Which concern debt obligations? | ANSWERED | Both ICICI Bank and Bajaj Finance explicitly consider debt obligations in their personal‑loan eligibility criteria (e.g., existing loans and credit‑card dues, and debt‑to‑income ratio). | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; policy source BFL-PL-OVERVIEW (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan> |
| 240 | Which concern employment? | ANSWERED | The policy rule that concerns employment is MIN_EMPLOYMENT_TENURE, which requires continuous employment of at least 12 months. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule MIN_EMPLOYMENT_TENURE (reason code ERR_EMPLOYMENT_TENURE_SHORT); policy source SYN-POLICY-V1 (approved) <repo://data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md> |
| 241 | Which concern loan amount? | ANSWERED | The loan amount is covered in Bajaj Finance’s Detailed Terms and Conditions (BFL‑PL‑DETAILED‑TNC), which state that the loan amount applied for/availed shall be the amount specified in the Application Form or MITC. | policy source BFL-PL-DETAILED-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/content/dam/bajajfinserv/web/in/en/global/document/one-web/adp/compliance/detailed-tncs-applicable-for-personal-loan.pdf> |
| 242 | Which concern loan tenure? | ANSWERED | The synthetic policy contains no rule about loan tenure; loan tenure is only addressed in the lender’s public policy (Bajaj Finance’s personal‑loan overview specifies tenures of 12‑108 months) and is validated by the underwriting schema (must be 1‑360 months). | `decision_engine/schemas/underwriting.py`; policy source BFL-PL-OVERVIEW (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan> |
| 243 | Which concern age? | ANSWERED | Age is a concern in the personal‑loan eligibility criteria; the policies explicitly set minimum and maximum ages (e.g., 18‑58 years for salaried ICICI applicants and 21‑80 years for Bajaj Finance applicants). | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; policy source BFL-PL-ELIG-DOCS (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan-eligibility-and-documents> |
| 244 | Which concern previous borrowing? | ANSWERED | The lender policies that address previous borrowing are ICICI (which asks borrowers to manage existing EMIs and keep debt levels low), HDFC (which looks at repayment history of prior loans), and Bajaj Finance (which flags high existing obligations as a rejection factor). | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; policy source HDFC-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.hdfcbank.com/personal/borrow/popular-loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; policy source BFL-INSIGHT-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/insights/4-reasons-for-personal-loan-rejection-and-how-to-avoid-them> |
| 245 | Which concern repayment history? | ANSWERED | Both HDFC Bank and ICICI Bank explicitly cite repayment history as a factor in personal‑loan eligibility. | policy source HDFC-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.hdfcbank.com/personal/borrow/popular-loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility> |
| 246 | Which concern reapplication? | ANSWERED | The reapplication concern is captured as the “Reapplication/what‑if” concern. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 3. P1 requirements |
| 247 | Are there policy exceptions? | ANSWERED | Yes. The RBI Digital Lending Directions list specific exceptions: guarantees under schemes such as CGTMSE, CRGFTLIH, NCGTC and guarantees from BIS, IMF or multilateral development banks are excluded from the definition of DLG. | policy source RBI-DLD-2025 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12848&Mode=0> |
| 248 | How are exceptions represented? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how exceptions are represented in the system’s policy model; clarification is needed from the project team or lender. **Who must answer:** a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 249 | Are policies product-specific? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 250 | Are policies applicant-segment-specific? | ANSWERED | Yes – the lender policies are applicant‑segment‑specific, adjusting rates and spreads based on the borrower’s business segment and profile. | policy source BFL-IRP-2023 (approved_historical) <https://cms-assets.bajajfinserv.in/is/content/bajajfinance/interest-rate-policy-v1pdf?fmt=pdf&scl=1> |
| 251 | Are policies location-specific? | ANSWERED | Yes. The lender policies explicitly include location‑specific factors (e.g., “Location delinquency and collection performance” in the interest‑rate variable list) and allow location‑wise display of information. | policy source BFL-PL-DETAILED-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/content/dam/bajajfinserv/web/in/en/global/document/one-web/adp/compliance/detailed-tncs-applicable-for-personal-loan.pdf>; policy source RBI-RBC-CB-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/170MD.PDF> |
| 252 | Are policies time-dependent? | ANSWERED | Yes. The policies include effective dates and can be amended over time, with revisions taking effect prospectively. | policy source BFL-PL-DETAILED-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/content/dam/bajajfinserv/web/in/en/global/document/one-web/adp/compliance/detailed-tncs-applicable-for-personal-loan.pdf>; policy source BFL-FPC-2026 (approved) <https://cms-assets.bajajfinserv.in/is/content/bajajfinance/fair-practice-code-ver-7-1-jan-2026-english?fmt=pdf&scl=1> |
| 253 | How are policy versions tracked? | ANSWERED | Policy versions are tracked in a versioned policy registry (registry.json) that maps each policy ID to specific versions, each pinned by SHA‑256 hashes of the policy and JDM files and includes metadata such as status and effective dates; load_policy() resolves the active version and validates integrity. | `decision_engine/policies/registry.json`; `decision_engine/policies/registry.py`; `tests/test_p3_workflow.py` |
| 254 | Which policy version applies to a historical application? | ANSWERED | A historical application is evaluated with the policy version that was pinned (active) at the time of the decision, which is stored in the decision‑evidence record’s policy_version field. | `decision_engine/policies/registry.py`; `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 255 | Can the policy change after the applicant was rejected? | UNKNOWN | The project documents show that policy content can be updated (a change creates a review event and does not automatically alter approval status), but they do not state whether such a change can retroactively affect a loan decision that was already rejected. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 256 | Should the agent explain the policy that existed at decision time or the current policy? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not state whether the agent should explain the policy that existed at decision time or the current policy. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 257 | What happens if the policy cannot answer the applicant's question? | ANSWERED | If the policy cannot answer, the agent abstains and returns a fallback template (policy_abstain or policy_busy) with a guard flag and no citations. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; `tests/test_agent_upgrades.py` |
| 258 | What happens if the policy is ambiguous? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what happens when the policy is ambiguous; this must be answered by the lender or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 259 | What happens if two policy documents conflict? | ANSWERED | If two policy documents conflict, the system keeps both entries and surfaces the conflict to the user rather than arbitrarily selecting one. | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources) |
| 260 | What happens if the policy contains contradictory rules? | PARTIAL | When contradictory policy rules are detected, the system surfaces the conflict (e.g., in the answer) rather than silently picking one side; such cases are flagged for manual review or reported as a conflict. | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 261 | What happens if a policy is outdated? | ANSWERED | If a policy is outdated—e.g., expired, superseded, or its file hash no longer matches the registry—the system fails safely: load_policy raises a PolicyIntegrityError, the policy is not loaded, and the agent excludes it from current answers, abstaining or returning no result. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 4 --- RAG implementation and evaluation; `decision_engine/policies/registry.py` |
| 262 | How do we prevent the LLM from inventing policy rules? | ANSWERED | We keep all underwriting rules inside the deterministic ZEN engine and never embed them in prompts or the agent, and the agent only echoes recorded rule evidence while guard logic blocks any ungrounded or invented policy statements. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 263 | How do we verify that a retrieved policy rule is actually relevant? | ANSWERED | The system verifies relevance in two stages. Retrieval filters passages by approval status, lender ID, entity type and source type before ranking; a post-hoc citation step then keeps only passages whose text supports the answer. `AGENT_CITATION_MODE` selects the check: `labels`, `verify`, `spans`, `spans_verify`, `llm_verify`, `primary_source`, `one_source` or `one_source_soft`. The modes do not all give the same guarantee: `verify`/`spans_verify` use lexical support, `llm_verify` asks a model, and `primary_source` keeps the cited passages from the single best-ranked source document (with historical/version-contrast handling), while `one_source`/`one_source_soft` constrain the composer to one document. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/settings.py` § `CITATION_MODES`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/citations.py` § Modes; `rag/retrieve.py` |
| 264 | Should policy retrieval be deterministic, semantic, or hybrid? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 265 | How do we cite the exact policy evidence used by the agent? | ANSWERED | The agent cites the exact policy evidence by attaching the policy metadata (policy ID, version, SHA‑256 hashes, threshold source, effective dates) and the rule‑level details (rule ID, name, version, reason code, field, operator, threshold, threshold source, description) in the Decision Evidence record, which the agent can reference in its response. | `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`); `docs/prototypes/p0-data-mapping.md` § ZEN output → evidence (rule join in Python) |

## 10. ELIGIBILITY VS APPROVAL

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 266 | What does "eligible" mean in the lender's context? | ANSWERED | In the lender’s context, “eligible” means the applicant satisfies the lender’s publicly stated eligibility criteria—such as age, income, credit score, nationality and employment status—making them a candidate for further underwriting, though final approval still requires detailed review. | policy source BFL-PL-ELIG-DOCS (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan-eligibility-and-documents>; policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility> |
| 267 | What does "approved" mean? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not define what the "approved" status means; only that documents can have an approval_status of "approved" as a metadata flag. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 268 | Are eligibility and approval separate concepts? | ANSWERED | Yes. Eligibility is a preliminary check of whether an applicant meets the lender’s criteria, while approval is the final decision after internal assessment and verification. | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility> |
| 269 | Can someone meet stated eligibility criteria and still be rejected? | PARTIAL | The materials indicate that meeting the publicly stated eligibility criteria does not guarantee approval, as lenders have internal underwriting rules not disclosed on public pages. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 3 --- policy document collection and governance |
| 270 | Can someone fail one apparent criterion but still be approved under an exception? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not indicate whether an applicant can fail a listed eligibility criterion and still be approved via an exception; only the explicit criteria are shown. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 271 | What should the agent say when an applicant asks, "Am I eligible?" | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 272 | Can the agent ever say that an applicant meets a specific eligibility criterion? | ANSWERED | Yes. The agent may state that an applicant meets a specific eligibility criterion when answering an applicant‑specific eligibility question, using authorized evidence and the applicable policy (not inventing any outcome). | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries |
| 273 | Can the agent ever say that an applicant appears eligible? | PARTIAL | The materials indicate the agent only consumes decision evidence and does not embed underwriting rules, so it is not designed to state eligibility, but there is no explicit statement that it can never say an applicant appears eligible. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 274 | Can the agent say that an applicant will be approved? | ANSWERED | No – the agent is explicitly prohibited from saying an applicant will be approved; it must abstain and state it cannot promise or predict approval. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 275 | Can the agent predict approval? | ANSWERED | No – the agent is explicitly designed to never predict or promise loan approval; it will respond with a guard such as “I can't promise or predict approval …”. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries |
| 276 | If prediction is technically possible, should the system provide it? | ANSWERED | No – even if a prediction is technically possible, the system must not provide it; it must refuse and state it cannot promise or predict approval. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks |
| 277 | How do we maintain the distinction between guidance and underwriting? | ANSWERED | The project keeps underwriting strictly inside the ZEN decision engine and lets the advisory agent only consume the resulting decision evidence to provide guidance, never embedding underwriting rules in prompts or the agent. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks |
| 278 | How do we prevent applicants from interpreting "eligible" as "approved"? | PARTIAL | The project includes guard clauses and agent boundaries that refuse to promise or imply approval (e.g., “I can't promise or predict approval …”) and explicitly forbid the agent from promising approval, which helps limit misinterpretation, but no specific UI or communication strategy is detailed. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries |
| 279 | What wording should be used to communicate this distinction? | ANSWERED | Use the guard wording that explicitly states the agent cannot promise or guarantee approval, e.g., “I can’t promise or predict approval…”, and also note it cannot approve or change a decision. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
