|---|---|---|---|---|
| 280 | What does a useful remediation plan look like? | ANSWERED | A useful remediation plan is a list of concrete steps, each targeting a specific failed rule and showing the required change, whether that rule would pass after the change, and the simulated decision outcome, with an optional combined‑changes scenario. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py`; `tests/e2e/chat_smoke.mjs` § chat_smoke.mjs |
| 281 | Which rejection factors are actually remediable? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify which rejection factors are remediable; the project lists the rule inputs but does not indicate which can be acted upon. **Who must answer:** a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 282 | Which factors are not immediately remediable? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify which factors are not immediately remediable; this requires input from the lender or project team. **Who must answer:** a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 283 | Which factors can the applicant change directly? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify which of the decision factors (income, FOIR, credit score, job tenure) the applicant can directly change; this must be answered by the lender or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 284 | Which factors require time? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 285 | Which factors require third-party correction? | PARTIAL | Only credit‑report (credit score) errors need to be corrected by a third party – the applicant is directed to dispute them with the credit bureau. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 286 | Which factors require lender intervention? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 287 | Which actions are explicitly supported by policy? | ANSWERED | The policy explicitly supports actions such as recovering bounce charges, penal interest and other penal charges; exercising legal rights and remedies (e.g., under Section 138 NI Act and Section 25 Payment and Settlement Systems Act); stipulating additional conditions or taking further actions as deemed fit; requiring the customer to indemnify the lender for claims, damages or losses; and cooperating to recover or reverse any incorrect disbursement. | policy source BFL-PL-DETAILED-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/content/dam/bajajfinserv/web/in/en/global/document/one-web/adp/compliance/detailed-tncs-applicable-for-personal-loan.pdf> |
| 288 | Which actions are general financial advice rather than lender-specific remediation? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not list which remediation actions are general financial advice versus lender‑specific steps. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 289 | Should the agent provide general financial education? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design explicitly includes providing general financial education (e.g., FOIR education) as part of the agent’s guidance, without requiring personal application data. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Proposed graph states/nodes; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 290 | Where is the boundary between general education and lender-specific advice? | ANSWERED | The system treats generic policy queries as general education (handled by policy retrieval), and only provides lender‑specific advice when the user asks about their own eligibility, returning authorized evidence plus the applicable policy. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |
| 291 | Can the agent recommend reducing debt? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not state that the advisory agent is designed to recommend reducing debt; they only contain external policy advice about debt impact. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 292 | Can the agent recommend waiting before reapplying? | N/A | The agent does not currently recommend waiting before reapplying; it only provides improvement steps based on failed rules, and waiting is not part of its advice. | `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Labels and counterfactuals |
| 293 | Can the agent recommend correcting inaccurate credit information? | ANSWERED | Yes. The agent cannot edit the record itself, but it can recommend correction by handing the request to a human reviewer, using a fixed refusal that says it will pass a correction request. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 294 | Can the agent recommend increasing income? | PARTIAL | The agent’s improvement plan can suggest changes to meet any failed rule, so it could recommend increasing income if the income threshold failed, but the materials do not show an explicit income‑increase recommendation. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 295 | Can the agent recommend changing the requested loan amount? | ANSWERED | No. The agent is prohibited from overwriting original application facts, so it cannot recommend changing the requested loan amount. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § 1. Project objective and product definition |
| 296 | Can the agent recommend changing the loan tenure? | N/A | The agent is designed only to explain underwriting decisions and does not have the capability to recommend changes to loan tenure. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 297 | Can the agent recommend paying credit-card balances? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not show the agent explicitly recommending paying credit‑card balances; they only show generic EMI reduction advice. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 298 | Under what evidence would each recommendation be valid? | ANSWERED | Each recommendation is valid only when it is grounded in the Decision Evidence layer – i.e. the failed‑rule IDs recorded in the evidence record and referenced via `evidence_refs[]` – and, where applicable, backed by policy‑corpus citations. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Output contract (proposal); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 299 | How specific should the action plan be? | ANSWERED | The action plan must be concrete and rule‑by‑rule, listing each failed underwriting rule with a specific numeric target and the gap to close (e.g., a target of ₹14,400 and a gap of ₹4,100 for the FOIR rule). | `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 300 | Can the agent state exact target values? | ANSWERED | Yes. The agent’s improvement plan includes exact target values and gaps, e.g., it can state a target of ₹14,400 and a gap of ₹4,100 for the FOIR rule. | `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 301 | Under what conditions can the agent calculate those target values? | ANSWERED | The agent only calculates target values when it is generating an improvement plan for a rejected application – i.e., for each FAILED rule it computes the smallest single‑field change (the target) and then re‑checks that change (and the combined changes) with ZEN’s `simulate` to ensure the rule would pass. The calculation is gated by the failed‑rule condition and ZEN simulation validation. | `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 302 | How should the agent handle actions that may improve one criterion but affect another? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 303 | Can the agent prioritize remediation steps? | PARTIAL | The agent generates an improvement plan listing a numbered task for each failed rule, but the materials do not describe any ordering or prioritisation of those steps. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; PR #6 (feat/langgraph-agent): Phase 5: single LangGraph advisory agent over ZEN evidence, Frappe and policy RAG (explains, never decides) § What is in it |
| 304 | How should it prioritize them? | ANSWERED | Prioritize dataset acquisition and audit first (Phase 1, immediate priority), then move to policy document collection and governance (Phase 3), followed by later automation and agent work. | policy source RBI-RBC-CB-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/170MD.PDF>; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § 0. Instructions for the next AI assistant |
| 305 | Should the agent estimate how difficult a remediation step is? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the agent should estimate the difficulty of a remediation step; this decision must be provided by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 306 | Should the agent estimate how long remediation may take? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether the agent should estimate remediation duration; this decision requires input from the lender or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 307 | What should happen if the policy provides no remediation path? | PARTIAL | If the policy does not include a remediation path, the agent should not suggest remediation steps and will only explain the rejection reasons. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § 1. Project objective and product definition |

## 12. COUNTERFACTUAL / "WHAT-IF" QUESTIONS

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 308 | What kinds of "what-if" questions should the agent support? | ANSWERED | The agent should only support what‑if questions that modify the four scored fields – monthly_income, monthly_obligations, credit_score, and employment_months – using numeric absolute or delta values within allowed ranges. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 309 | Can the applicant change income in a simulation? | ANSWERED | Yes – the what‑if simulation permits the applicant to change the monthly_income field (one of the four allowed scored fields) within its defined numeric limits. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py` |
| 310 | Can the applicant change monthly debt? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system permits a what‑if change to the applicant’s monthly debt (monthly_obligations) as one of the four allowed fields, subject to the defined numeric limits. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 311 | Can the applicant change loan amount? | ANSWERED | No – the applicant cannot change the loan amount; only the lender may revise the sanctioned amount at its sole discretion. | policy source BFL-PL-DETAILED-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/content/dam/bajajfinserv/web/in/en/global/document/one-web/adp/compliance/detailed-tncs-applicable-for-personal-loan.pdf> |
| 312 | Can the applicant change loan tenure? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 313 | Can the applicant change employment tenure? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether an applicant can change their employment tenure; only the rule and field mapping are defined. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 314 | Can the applicant change credit score? | ANSWERED | Yes – the what‑if tool lets the applicant propose a hypothetical change to their credit score (along with the other three scored fields), but the result is only a simulation, not a real change or guarantee. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/frappe_adapter.py` |
| 315 | Can the applicant remove a credit-card balance? | ANSWERED | No – the agent cannot delete or edit a credit‑card balance; such requests are blocked and handed off to a human reviewer, only a what‑if simulation is possible. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 316 | Can the applicant change multiple variables simultaneously? | PARTIAL | The what‑if tool permits changes only to the four scored fields, each within a numeric range, and each change is evaluated independently; the documentation does not specify whether multiple fields can be altered together. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 317 | Which variables can be safely simulated? | ANSWERED | The only variables that can be safely simulated are the four scored fields – monthly income, monthly obligations, credit score, and employment months – each within its defined numeric range. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py` |
| 318 | Which variables cannot be safely simulated? | ANSWERED | Any variable that is not one of the four scored fields (minimum monthly income, FOIR, credit score, months in current job) cannot be safely simulated; attempts to change other fields are rejected. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py` |
| 319 | Which calculations should be performed by deterministic code? | ANSWERED | All underwriting rule calculations, input validation, mapping to the canonical schema, and what‑if simulations must be performed by deterministic code (the ZEN engine), while the LLM only explains the results. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 320 | Which parts, if any, require an LLM? | ANSWERED | The only components that need an LLM are the advisory agent’s language‑generation steps – the graph’s compose/what‑if and policy‑composition calls that invoke self.llm.chat or self.llm.chat_stream – and the voice‑pipeline LLM node that produces the streamed answer sentences for TTS. All underwriting logic, data mapping and retrieval are deterministic and do not require an LLM. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; `apps/voice/agent_worker.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks |
| 321 | How should hypothetical assumptions be represented? | ANSWERED | Hypothetical assumptions must be expressed only for the four scored fields (monthly_income, monthly_obligations, credit_score, employment_months) within their numeric limits, using a JSON‑like set/delta format, and the simulation is flagged as a non‑guaranteed, synthetic‑policy result. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py`; `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Labels and counterfactuals |
| 322 | How should the agent distinguish simulation from reality? | ANSWERED | The agent must tag what‑if outputs with the `is_simulation=True` flag, include explicit disclaimer language that it is a simulation under the synthetic policy and not a guarantee, and ensure the original application data remains unchanged. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 323 | How should the agent communicate counterfactual uncertainty? | ANSWERED | The agent must label the what‑if output as a simulation, explicitly state it is a hypothetical result and not a decision or guarantee, and remind the user that a lender decides on a fresh application. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 324 | Should the system calculate the minimum change required to satisfy a policy threshold? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system computes the smallest single‑field change needed to meet each policy threshold, using its counterfactual logic. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/facts.py`; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Counterfactual explanations |
| 325 | How should it handle multiple thresholds simultaneously? | PARTIAL | The prototype’s what‑if tool re‑runs ZEN with overridden inputs and reports per‑rule before/after results; it can also handle a combined simulation (via an “all_changes_together” flag) that would meet all four synthetic thresholds, but the design only explicitly details single‑field changes. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/facts.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 326 | What happens when satisfying one condition still leaves another failed condition? | ANSWERED | A what‑if simulation re‑runs ZEN with the changed input, marks the rule that now passes, and still reports any other rules that remain failed; the original decision record is left unchanged. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Labels and counterfactuals |
| 327 | What happens when changing one variable changes another variable? | ANSWERED | Changing one variable does not cause other variables to be recomputed; each of the four scored fields is treated as an independent input in the what‑if simulation. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 328 | Can counterfactual calculations reproduce the actual underwriting system? | ANSWERED | Yes – the counterfactual ‘what‑if’ tool re‑runs the same ZEN deterministic underwriting engine with the altered inputs, so it reproduces the actual underwriting system’s logic. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 329 | If not, how should the system communicate that limitation? | PARTIAL | The system should surface its limitation via the guard/fallback path – returning a clear “cannot answer” response (e.g., draft = null with guard flags) and appending a label that the explanation is based on synthetic policy thresholds. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `docs/agent/README.md` § Phase 5 — Loan Advisory Agent (one LangGraph graph) + Phase 6 voice |
| 330 | Should counterfactual results be stored? | ANSWERED | No, counterfactual results are not stored; they are simulated in memory only and the original record remains unchanged. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 331 | Should counterfactual results become part of conversation memory? | ANSWERED | No – counterfactual (what‑if) results are kept only in transient memory and are not persisted into conversation memory, which is never treated as authoritative loan data. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 332 | Can a counterfactual result ever be interpreted as an approval guarantee? | ANSWERED | No. Counterfactual results are explicitly marked as simulations and are never presented as approval guarantees; the system labels them as "not a decision and not a guarantee" and guards block guarantee language. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 333 | How do we prevent that? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |

## 13. REAPPLICATION

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 334 | What determines whether an applicant should reapply? | PARTIAL | The prototype uses a synthetic policy rule that a rejected applicant can reapply within 7 days, and the counterfactual data includes a `months_to_wait` field that indicates how long to wait before reapplying. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-INJECT-002.md` § Approval guarantee; `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Labels and counterfactuals |
| 335 | Does the lender have a formal reapplication waiting period? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not provide a definitive answer about whether the lender has a formal reapplication waiting period; the synthetic policy notes no waiting period, but that is not a real lender policy. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 336 | Does the waiting period differ by product? | ANSWERED | The synthetic policy explicitly states that no waiting period is defined, so there is no product‑specific waiting period. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Outcome |
| 337 | Does the waiting period differ by rejection reason? | ANSWERED | The synthetic policy does not define any waiting period, so there is no waiting period that varies by rejection reason. | policy source SYN-POLICY-V1 (approved) <repo://data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md>; policy source RBI-MC-CS-2015 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_ViewMasCirculardetails.aspx?id=9862> |
| 338 | Does the waiting period differ by applicant? | ANSWERED | The synthetic policy states there is no waiting period defined for reapplication, so it does not vary by applicant. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Outcome; policy source SYN-POLICY-V1 (approved) <repo://data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md> |
| 339 | What should happen if there is no stated waiting period? | ANSWERED | Because the synthetic policy explicitly states it does not define any waiting period before reapplying, the system should not enforce a waiting period and the applicant may reapply immediately. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Outcome; policy source SYN-POLICY-V1 (approved) <repo://data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md> |
| 340 | Can the agent calculate the earliest possible reapplication date? | PARTIAL | The current prototype does not calculate the earliest reapplication date; a `get_reapplication_guidance` tool is only proposed for future development. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Proposed initial tools |
| 341 | What conditions must be satisfied before reapplication? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify any concrete conditions that must be satisfied before a rejected applicant can reapply; the project only mentions that reapplication guidance will be explored when supported by policy, but no specific criteria are given. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 342 | Can all conditions be checked by the agent? | ANSWERED | No. The agent does not check underwriting conditions; they are evaluated only by the ZEN engine and the agent merely explains the resulting evidence. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; PR #6 (feat/langgraph-agent): Phase 5: single LangGraph advisory agent over ZEN evidence, Frappe and policy RAG (explains, never decides) § Phase 5: one LangGraph advisory graph over ZEN evidence, Frappe and the policy RAG |
| 343 | Which conditions require new data? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify which reapplication conditions require new data; the project team or the lender must define these conditions. **Who must answer:** a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 344 | Which conditions can only be evaluated during underwriting? | PARTIAL | The underwriting engine evaluates only the four synthetic criteria: monthly income ≥ ₹25 k, FOIR/DTI ≤ 45 %, credit score ≥ 700, and employment tenure ≥ 12 months. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 2 --- data pipeline, conventional automation and decision engine; `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 345 | What should the agent say if the applicant appears ready but approval remains uncertain? | ANSWERED | The agent must state it cannot promise or predict approval and that the outcome remains uncertain, using the no‑decision/uncertainty template and avoiding any guarantee language. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 346 | What should the agent say if the applicant is clearly not ready? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 347 | What should the agent say if the agent cannot determine readiness? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what the agent should say when it cannot determine readiness. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 348 | Should the agent actively discourage premature reapplication? | PARTIAL | The system is built to block any guarantee of approval for a quick re‑application (tests filter out “definitely” language), but there is no explicit rule that the agent must actively discourage a premature re‑application; only the public policy note hints that repeated enquiries may be undesirable. | `tests/e2e/chat_smoke.mjs` § chat_smoke.mjs; `tests/test_agent_upgrades.py`; policy source BFL-PL-OVERVIEW (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan> |
| 349 | Can the agent explain the cost or consequences of repeated applications? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify that the agent can explain the cost or consequences of repeated applications; this requires lender‑specific policy or data. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 350 | What information should be provided before reapplication? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify which information an applicant must provide before reapplying; this detail must be supplied by the lender or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 351 | Should the agent provide a checklist before reapplication? | PARTIAL | The policy recommends applicants keep a checklist, and the project plans an optional re‑application guidance tool that could include such a checklist, but the materials do not state a definitive decision that the agent must provide one. | policy source BFL-INSIGHT-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/insights/4-reasons-for-personal-loan-rejection-and-how-to-avoid-them>; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Proposed initial tools |
| 352 | Can the applicant ask the agent to re-check readiness later? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether an applicant can ask the agent to re‑check readiness later; clarification from the team is needed. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |

## 14. MULTI-TURN CONVERSATION

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 353 | What information should be remembered across turns? | ANSWERED | The agent must retain the conversation state across turns, specifically the current intent, any necessary prior turns, the clarification state, tool outputs, and the thread/checkpointer reference; loan data stays in the authoritative Frappe/Evidence services. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 354 | Should the applicant's identity be remembered? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system retains the applicant’s identity (applicant_ref) across turns, but only the minimal necessary PII, with strict access control and retention policies. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/server.py`; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § A. Applicant identity/reference |
| 355 | Should the application ID be remembered? | ANSWERED | No – the application ID is not kept as authoritative conversation memory; each turn fetches the loan state from Frappe and the thread ID is only for scoping, not for authorizing decisions. | `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 356 | Should previous explanations be remembered? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system keeps prior turns in the conversation state so earlier explanations can be recalled for context, but this memory is never treated as authoritative loan data. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 357 | Should already-explained concepts be remembered? | PARTIAL | The project stores conversation state, including prior turns, so the agent can reference earlier explanations, but there is no explicit policy stating that already‑explained concepts must be remembered. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 358 | Should previously simulated scenarios be remembered? | ANSWERED | No – the system does not retain simulated what‑if scenarios; they are run in‑memory and never persisted across turns. | PR #6 (feat/langgraph-agent): Phase 5: single LangGraph advisory agent over ZEN evidence, Frappe and policy RAG (explains, never decides) § Phase 5: one LangGraph advisory graph over ZEN evidence, Frappe and the policy RAG; `docs/agent/README.md` § Phase 5 — Loan Advisory Agent (one LangGraph graph) + Phase 6 voice |
| 359 | Should the applicant's stated preferences be remembered? | PARTIAL | The system can keep an applicant’s stated preferences in the transient conversation state, but it must not treat them as authoritative loan data or persist them beyond what is necessary, especially avoiding unnecessary PII. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 360 | How should the agent resolve pronouns such as "it", "that", and "this"? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify how the agent should resolve pronouns such as "it", "that", and "this". **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 361 | How should the agent handle follow-up questions that depend on previous turns? | ANSWERED | The agent must keep a per‑applicant thread (session) and store conversation state (current intent, prior turns, tool outputs, thread/checkpointer reference) in LangGraph’s MemorySaver, using that context to answer follow‑up questions while never treating the memory as authoritative loan data. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 362 | How should the agent handle a topic switch? | UNKNOWN | The provided documentation does not describe how the agent should handle a topic switch; the project team must define this behavior. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 363 | How should the agent handle contradictory information across turns? | PARTIAL | The agent is designed to surface contradictory information rather than resolve it arbitrarily – it keeps both conflicting statements and cites the source pages, avoiding a single definitive claim. This guidance is documented for policy conflicts, but the handling of contradictory user statements across turns is not fully specified. | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources) |
| 364 | Which conversation state should be structured rather than stored as raw text? | ANSWERED | The conversation state—including the current intent, necessary prior turns, clarification state, tool outputs, and thread/checkpointer reference—should be stored in a structured form rather than as raw text. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 365 | How much conversation history is necessary? | PARTIAL | The agent keeps only the current intent and the prior turns that are required for clarification and tool use; it does not retain the full conversation transcript. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 366 | When should old information be discarded? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 367 | What happens if the applicant returns after a long period? | PARTIAL | The demo session token issued to the applicant expires after four hours (exp = time.time() + 4 * 3600), so returning after a long period requires a new sign‑in; the in‑memory conversation thread is not persisted as authoritative loan data, so prior turns may be lost. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/server.py`; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 368 | Should old policy information remain attached to old conversations? | ANSWERED | No – old policy information should be kept in a separate versioned store for audit/historical analysis, not attached to conversation memory. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 4 --- RAG implementation and evaluation |
| 369 | What happens when applicant data changes during the conversation? | ANSWERED | The agent never updates the applicant’s record; if the user claims a data error, the system runs a what‑if simulation or escalates via a handoff, informing the user that a human reviewer will check the data. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 370 | What happens when the lender updates the application? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 371 | How should the agent tell the applicant that information has changed? | ANSWERED | The agent must not claim to have changed any applicant data; it should tell the user it cannot edit the record and can only run a what‑if scenario or forward the correction to a human reviewer. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
