## 16. PERSONALIZATION

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 385 | What does "personalized explanation" mean for this product? | PARTIAL | The materials show that the product’s explanation is built from the applicant‑specific decision evidence together with the relevant policy, which implies a personalized explanation, but they do not give a formal definition of the term. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Target later architecture |
| 386 | Is personalization based on applicant data? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system’s personalization is driven by applicant data, as the policy explicitly allows using applicant information for personalization of products or services. | policy source BFL-KYC-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/tnc-my-account-kyc> |
| 387 | Is personalization based on the current application? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system personalizes decisions using only the data from the current application; historical data is not used for underwriting. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 11. Historical data; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 388 | Is personalization based on conversation history? | PARTIAL | The project defines a conversation‑state memory (e.g., prior turns, intent) but explicitly says it is not used as authoritative loan data, and it does not state that personalization of explanations is derived from that history. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 389 | Is personalization based on knowledge level? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify whether personalization is based on the applicant’s knowledge level; this detail would need to be supplied by the project team or the lender. **Who must answer:** a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 390 | Is personalization based on language? | ANSWERED | Yes – the agent personalizes its output by language, selecting the locale (en/hi/te) for the response style. | `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 391 | Is personalization based on communication preferences? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system personalizes explanations using communication preferences such as the applicant’s chosen literacy level, language, and channel (voice), as reflected in the literacy and locale parameters and the communication‑adaptation evaluation. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `apps/voice/agent_worker.py` |
| 392 | Which applicant characteristics should never affect the substance of the explanation? | PARTIAL | Only the answer language, the applicant‑chosen literacy level, and the applicant’s own recorded facts may affect the explanation; all other applicant characteristics (e.g., gender, age, religion, caste, etc.) should not affect its substance. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 393 | Should the same rejection reason always produce the same underlying factual explanation? | ANSWERED | Yes. Because the underwriting engine is deterministic and the evidence record stores only the policy’s reason codes and descriptions, the same rejection reason under the same policy version will always generate the same factual explanation. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 394 | How can presentation change without changing the underlying facts? | ANSWERED | The system separates presentation (style, language, voice) from the factual decision evidence, so you can vary how the explanation is rendered—changing style, language or voice templates—while the underlying facts (the JSON facts block) stay unchanged. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 6 --- LiveKit voice; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 395 | Can two applicants receive differently worded explanations for the same policy rule? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system can vary wording by answer language and applicant‑chosen literacy level, so two applicants may receive differently worded explanations for the same rule. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 396 | How do we ensure personalization does not alter the decision facts? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 397 | How do we test whether personalization is actually useful? | PARTIAL | The project includes an A2 literacy‑pair test that compares low‑vs‑high‑literacy explanations for the same persona, but it does not measure whether the personalized wording improves user understanding or outcomes; that would require real‑applicant user research. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |

## 17. QUESTION INTENT / ROUTING

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 398 | Should the system classify every applicant question before answering? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design requires every applicant question to be classified first; the StateGraph pipeline begins with a classify step before any evidence loading, policy retrieval, or response generation. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`) |
| 399 | What question categories do we need? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 400 | How accurately must question classification work? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify a required accuracy level for question classification; the team must define this requirement. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 401 | What happens when a question belongs to multiple categories? | N/A | The system’s categories are defined as disjoint, so a question never belongs to multiple categories. | `tests/test_abstain_set.py` |
| 402 | What happens when intent is ambiguous? | ANSWERED | If the intent is ambiguous the fast classifier returns None and the system defers classification to the LLM; if the LLM cannot classify (e.g., budget exceeded) it falls back to the policy path. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; `tests/test_agent_upgrades.py` |
| 403 | Should the agent ask a clarifying question? | ANSWERED | Yes – the agent is designed to ask clarifying questions whenever it lacks sufficient information or encounters ambiguous input, using the "clarify" action for statuses like no_decision, info_requested, or out‑of‑range what‑if. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |
| 404 | When should it answer directly? | ANSWERED | It should answer directly for rejection‑explanation requests, general policy questions, applicant‑specific eligibility queries, and what‑if simulations, as these can be satisfied with decision evidence, policy retrieval, or deterministic simulation without escalation. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |
| 405 | When should it retrieve applicant data? | ANSWERED | The system retrieves applicant data only for applicant‑specific intents such as eligibility questions, rejection explanations, or what‑if simulations (i.e., when authorized evidence is needed). It must not retrieve data on identity/access failures or for generic policy queries. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |
| 406 | When should it retrieve policy? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 407 | When should it perform a calculation? | PARTIAL | The system performs a calculation when handling a what‑if (hypothetical) request: it first validates the allowed input, then runs a deterministic ZEN simulation to produce the result. Other calculation triggers (e.g., during normal underwriting) are implied but not explicitly detailed in the materials. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |
| 408 | When should it use multiple tools? | PARTIAL | The agent can call more than one tool in a single turn (e.g., loading decision evidence then running a what‑if simulation), but the materials do not specify a concrete rule that dictates when multiple tools must be used. | `tests/test_agent_graph.py`; `docs/agent/README.md` § Tools (`tools.py`, the only things the graph can touch) |
| 409 | When should it hand off to a human? | ANSWERED | The system hands off to a human when there is a data dispute, the loan system is unreachable, the application is in manual review, or when an override/record edit or misrepresentation refusal occurs. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 410 | What happens when the applicant asks an unrelated question? | PARTIAL | The agent classifies an off‑topic or unrelated applicant message as intent "other" (see classification schema) and does not treat it as a policy, explain, improve or what‑if request; it falls back to a generic response or abstains rather than providing a loan‑related answer. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 411 | What happens when the applicant asks a question outside the lender's policy? | ANSWERED | The system routes the out‑of‑policy question to the policy retrieval path; the policy tool will either cite a relevant source or abstain (e.g., reply “Not in the sources.”). | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; `tests/test_agent_upgrades.py` |

## 18. OUT-OF-SCOPE QUESTIONS

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 412 | What questions are explicitly outside the system's role? | ANSWERED | The system must refuse or abstain from questions about invented internal thresholds, other lenders, guarantees, cross‑lender comparisons, future predictions, out‑of‑scope products, record‑tampering actions, injection attempts, and any personal or internal data (e.g., requests to change a rejected application). | `docs/agent/citation_accuracy.md` § Abstention set abstain_v2 (41 agent-authored unanswerable questions, 9 families) |
| 413 | What questions should always receive a refusal? | ANSWERED | The system refuses any request that matches the safety‑edge guards: guarantees of approval, asks to approve/override/reconsider a decision, attempts to fake, inflate, hide or forge information, requests for confidential internal data, prompt‑injection commands, or asks the agent to submit or re‑apply on the applicant’s behalf (including edit‑record tampering). | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `tests/test_misrepresentation_guard.py` |
| 414 | What questions should receive a human handoff? | ANSWERED | Any user query that involves a data dispute, the loan system being unreachable, an application in manual review, an override/record edit or misrepresentation, missing or conflicting evidence, identity/access failures, or a request to change/override a decision should be handed off to a human reviewer. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent |
| 415 | What questions can receive a generic educational answer? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify which questions are eligible for a generic educational answer; this decision must be provided by the project team (VaaniLabs). **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs |  |
| 416 | Can the agent answer general financial-literacy questions? | N/A | The agent is scoped only to explain decisions for rejected personal‑loan applicants; answering general financial‑literacy questions is outside the project’s scope. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Initial scope decisions; `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 7. Open items |
| 417 | Can the agent answer questions about other lenders? | ANSWERED | No – the agent must abstain from answering any question about a lender other than the configured profile lender. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/agent/citation_accuracy.md` § Abstention set abstain_v2 (41 agent-authored unanswerable questions, 9 families) |
| 418 | Can the agent compare loan products? | N/A | The agent does not compare loan products; it only explains a single personal‑loan decision and its rejection reasons. | memory: project note (loan-advisory-agent) § Loan Advisory Agent (VaaniLabs.AI capstone; repo Narendarcodes/Load-Advisory-Agent, "Load" spelling) |
| 419 | Can the agent recommend another lender? | N/A | The agent cannot recommend another lender because it is designed only to explain decisions and never makes decisions or recommendations. | PR #9 (feat/citation-accuracy): Citation accuracy: primary_source mode (opt-in), abstain_v2 set, record-tampering guards § What; run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 1. Scope and honesty constraints (verified in code/docs) |
| 420 | Can the agent recommend a specific financial product? | ANSWERED | No. The agent is designed only to explain decisions and never to decide or recommend a specific financial product. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 1. Scope and honesty constraints (verified in code/docs); `docs/agent/README.md` § Phase 5 — Loan Advisory Agent (one LangGraph graph) + Phase 6 voice |
| 421 | Can the agent provide investment advice? | N/A | The agent is limited to explaining personal‑loan decisions and never provides investment advice; such advice is out of scope. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 1. Scope and honesty constraints (verified in code/docs) |
| 422 | Can the agent provide tax advice? | PARTIAL | The agent is limited to explaining personal‑loan decisions and does not provide tax advice. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 1. Scope and honesty constraints (verified in code/docs) |
| 423 | Can the agent provide legal advice? | ANSWERED | No. The agent is limited to explaining loan decisions and the project explicitly states it does not give legal advice. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 1. Scope and honesty constraints (verified in code/docs); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Research note: regulation and published design references (question bank s.40, 41, 44, 45, 20, 22); policy source BFL-PRIVACY (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/privacy-policy> |
| 424 | Where should the system boundary be drawn? | ANSWERED | The system boundary is drawn at the decision‑evidence contract: the agent only consumes Decision Evidence and never accesses raw Frappe data or the database. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |

## 19. DATA CORRECTION / DISPUTES

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 425 | What should happen if the applicant says their salary is wrong? | ANSWERED | The system treats the claim as a data dispute, creates a handoff to a human reviewer via `HandoffQueue.create("data_dispute", …)`, and replies with a template stating the issue has been passed to a reviewer and cannot be edited. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 426 | What should happen if the applicant says their employment status is wrong? | ANSWERED | The agent must not edit the application; it creates a data‑dispute handoff to a human reviewer and replies that it cannot change the record. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 427 | What should happen if the applicant says their credit information is wrong? | ANSWERED | The system creates a data‑dispute handoff to a human reviewer and replies with a fixed template stating the request has been passed on and the record cannot be changed. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 428 | What should happen if the applicant says a loan does not belong to them? | ANSWERED | The system treats the claim as a data dispute, creates a handoff to a human reviewer via `HandoffQueue.create("data_dispute", ...)` and replies with the dispute template stating it cannot edit the record. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 429 | What should happen if the applicant says an EMI amount is incorrect? | ANSWERED | If an applicant claims the EMI amount is wrong, the system treats it as a data dispute, creates a handoff to a human reviewer and replies with a fixed refusal stating it cannot edit the record. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 430 | Can the agent modify applicant information? | ANSWERED | No, the agent cannot modify applicant information; it has no write tool for the application record and refuses edit requests. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 431 | If not, what can it do? | ANSWERED | The agent cannot edit the applicant’s record; instead it can run a what‑if simulation with alternative numbers without changing the stored data, or forward the correction request to a human reviewer via a handoff. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 432 | Can the agent initiate a correction request? | ANSWERED | Yes. The agent cannot edit the application itself, but it can initiate a correction request by creating a human‑review handoff (e.g., `HandoffQueue.create("data_dispute", …)`). | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 433 | Should the agent collect evidence for a correction request? | ANSWERED | The agent does not collect evidence for a correction request; it only stores the applicant's message (up to 500 characters) and does not ask for documents, leaving evidence collection to the lender/reviewer. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 434 | When should a human be involved? | ANSWERED | A human reviewer is involved when a data dispute is raised, when the loan system is unreachable, when the application is in manual review, or when an override/edit/misrepresentation request needs correction. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 435 | How should disputed information affect the explanation? | ANSWERED | Disputed information does not change the explanation; the agent routes the request to a human reviewer via a handoff and replies with a fixed template stating it cannot edit the record. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 436 | Should the agent explain that the current decision may have relied on disputed data? | ANSWERED | No. When an applicant disputes data, the agent does not explain that the current decision may have relied on that disputed information; it instead hands the request off to a human reviewer with a fixed refusal template. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |

## 20. PRIVACY

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 437 | What applicant information is private? | ANSWERED | The system treats applicant PAN numbers and OTP secrets as sensitive private data that are never stored or processed, and it does not expose internal grades, scores, or the identity of the file handler to the applicant. | `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § 2. Applicant records; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 438 | What applicant information can be shown back to the applicant? | ANSWERED | The applicant may be shown only their own recorded decision, the rule checks behind it, and the values they submitted; internal grades, scores, or handler identity are not shown. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 439 | What applicant information should not be repeated unnecessarily? | ANSWERED | The system should not repeat internal grades/scores or the identity of the staff who handled the file; only the decision, rule checks and the applicant’s submitted values are shown back. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 440 | Can the agent expose sensitive financial information in the conversation? | ANSWERED | No – the agent is designed not to expose sensitive financial information; it only returns the applicant’s decision, rule checks and submitted values, and omits financial values in its messages. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 441 | How should identity verification work? | ANSWERED | Identity verification should be done via Aadhaar‑based e‑KYC or offline KYC with the applicant’s consent, where the lender shares the Aadhaar number/biometrics with UIDAI/CIDR and receives authentication and demographic data for verification. | policy source BFL-KYC-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/tnc-my-account-kyc> |
| 442 | What happens if someone else gains access to an applicant's conversation? | ANSWERED | The system blocks any attempt to view another applicant’s conversation with an `other_applicant` guard that replies it can only discuss the user’s own application, and the lender’s privacy policy mandates prompt breach notification to the affected individual and authorities. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; policy source BFL-PRIVACY (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/privacy-policy> |
| 443 | Can an applicant ask about another person's application? | ANSWERED | No. The system’s `other_applicant` guard blocks such requests, responding that it can only discuss the applicant’s own application and cannot share or look up anyone else’s details. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 444 | Can an applicant ask what another person's credit score was? | ANSWERED | No – the agent will refuse and say it can only discuss the applicant’s own application and cannot share anyone else’s credit score. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 445 | What information about family members can be discussed? | ANSWERED | The policy permits discussing the family members’ contact details (e.g., phone numbers, email addresses) that the customer has provided, for purposes such as sending reminders about outstanding dues. | policy source BFL-PL-DETAILED-TNC (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/content/dam/bajajfinserv/web/in/en/global/document/one-web/adp/compliance/detailed-tncs-applicable-for-personal-loan.pdf> |
| 446 | What information about employers can be disclosed? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify which employer information may be disclosed; this can only be answered by the lender (Bajaj Finance) or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 447 | What information about internal lender systems can be disclosed? | ANSWERED | The lender’s privacy policy states that internal information and systems are protected and not disclosed publicly; they are only shared with third‑party service providers on a strict need‑to‑know basis. | policy source BFL-PRIVACY (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/privacy-policy> |
| 448 | What information should never be disclosed even to the applicant? | ANSWERED | The system must never disclose other applicants’ details, who handled the file, internal grades or scores, or any internal scoring models, rule thresholds, or cut‑offs to the applicant. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 449 | How long should conversation data be stored? | ANSWERED | The project does not define a fixed retention period; RBI Digital Lending Directions require each lender to set and disclose its own data-storage policy, so the lender (and VaaniLabs as provider) decide how long conversation data is kept. For tracing data specifically, `docs/observability/langfuse.md` § Data access, retention and deletion records that the local Langfuse store has no automatic retention window: traces are deleted through the UI or by removing the named volumes, and `LANGFUSE_CAPTURE_CONTENT` stays off outside a controlled demo. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § RBI (Digital Lending) Directions, 2025 (May 8, 2025); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; `docs/observability/langfuse.md` § Data access, retention and deletion |
| 450 | How long should applicant data be stored? | UNKNOWN | The project does not specify a retention period; the lender deploying the system (and VaaniLabs as provider) must decide how long applicant data is stored. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |
| 451 | What data should be deleted after the conversation? | PARTIAL | The project states that only necessary PII should be persisted and that conversation memory should avoid storing financial details, with logs keeping minimal metadata; however it does not define a concrete list of data to delete or a specific retention schedule. `docs/observability/langfuse.md` § Data access, retention and deletion distinguishes application data from tracing data: the Langfuse volumes hold spans (and conversations only when `LANGFUSE_CAPTURE_CONTENT` is on) and are deleted through the UI or by removing those volumes; conversation retention itself is still undefined. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; `docs/observability/langfuse.md` § Data access, retention and deletion |
| 452 | Which logs must be anonymized? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify which logs must be anonymized; the project states that the anonymisation policy is not set and must be decided by the lender and VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / a partner lender |  |

## 21. SECURITY / PROMPT INJECTION / ABUSE

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 453 | How could an applicant attempt to manipulate the agent? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 454 | Could an applicant attempt to override the system prompt? | ANSWERED | An applicant can try to inject instructions to override the system prompt, but the agent’s injection guard catches this and replies that its rules don’t change based on such messages. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 455 | Could an applicant ask the agent to reveal internal instructions? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 456 | Could an applicant ask for hidden policy rules? | ANSWERED | If an applicant asks for hidden policy rules, the system’s request guard flags it as confidential internal information and replies with a refusal, e.g., “That information isn’t available to me …”. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 457 | Could an applicant ask for internal model scores? | ANSWERED | No – the system will not disclose internal model scores; such requests are caught by the `not_available` guard and the agent replies that the information isn’t available. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 458 | Could an applicant ask how to bypass underwriting? | ANSWERED | No – the agent will refuse, invoking the misrepresentation guard and stating it cannot help hide, inflate, or cheat the underwriting system. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 459 | Could an applicant ask how to manipulate an application? | ANSWERED | Yes, an applicant could ask how to manipulate their application, but the system flags such requests as `misrepresentation` and returns a deterministic refusal template, refusing to provide any guidance. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `tests/test_misrepresentation_guard.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 460 | Could an applicant ask which values would produce approval? | ANSWERED | The agent will not reveal which values would guarantee approval; requests for guaranteed approval are caught by the `guarantee` guard and answered with a refusal such as “I can't promise or predict approval …”. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 461 | Could an applicant intentionally provide false information? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 462 | How should the agent respond? | ANSWERED | The agent must trigger the injection guard and refuse, replying with the template “My rules don’t change based on instructions in a message.” | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 463 | What prompt-injection attacks are relevant? | ANSWERED | The system guards against prompt‑injection attacks such as attempts to "ignore previous instructions", use a "system prompt", invoke "developer mode", claim "you are now" the lender, "pretend to be" the model, "jailbreak" it, or ask it to "reveal your prompt or instructions". | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 464 | What tool-injection attacks are relevant? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 465 | What data-poisoning risks are relevant? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 466 | How do we protect policy retrieval from manipulation? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 467 | How do we prevent sensitive internal information from appearing in generated responses? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 468 | How do we test the agent against adversarial prompts? | ANSWERED | The project tests adversarial prompts by using synthetic adversarial documents (e.g., SYN‑INJECT‑001) and unit tests that feed malicious inputs to a scripted LLM, then asserting that guard flags trigger, untrusted text is filtered, and fallback templates are used. | `data/sources/policy_register.yaml`; `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |

## 22. HUMAN HANDOFF

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 469 | Which situations require human intervention? | ANSWERED | Human intervention is required for data disputes, when the loan system (Frappe) is unreachable, when the application is in manual review, and for override/record‑edit or misrepresentation refusals. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 470 | Should the system have a formal escalation policy? | UNKNOWN | The materials describe a prototype handoff mechanism but do not state whether a formal escalation policy should be adopted; they note there is no real escalation channel yet. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 471 | What should trigger escalation? | ANSWERED | Escalation is triggered when there is a data dispute, the loan system (Frappe) is unreachable, the application is in manual review, or when a user attempts an override, record edit, or misrepresentation that requires human review. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 472 | Should policy ambiguity trigger escalation? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify that policy ambiguity should trigger a human escalation; the defined handoff triggers do not include this case. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 473 | Should data disputes trigger escalation? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system is designed to treat a data dispute as a trigger for human escalation, creating a handoff record. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 474 | Should fraud concerns trigger escalation? | ANSWERED | No – the system does not trigger a human handoff for fraud concerns; escalation on fraud signals is not built. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 475 | Should identity problems trigger escalation? | UNKNOWN | The materials specify that an identity/access failure causes the system to stop and not retrieve application data, but they do not explicitly state that such identity problems trigger a human escalation. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 476 | Should repeated failed explanations trigger escalation? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify that repeated failed explanations should trigger a human escalation; this decision must be supplied by the project team. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 477 | Should complaints trigger escalation? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not state that user complaints trigger a handoff escalation; the decision is not addressed and must be answered by the project team/VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 478 | How does the applicant know they have been handed off? | ANSWERED | The applicant is told they have been handed off through the agent’s reply text, which includes a handoff reference (e.g., HO‑xxxxxxxxxx), and the API response contains `action="handoff"` and a `handoff_ref`. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 479 | What information should be transferred to the human support agent? | ANSWERED | The handoff transfers the issue category, the application reference, and the applicant’s message; the reviewer can then retrieve the full decision evidence using the application reference, with the audit log providing the execution trace. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 480 | Should the conversation transcript be transferred? | ANSWERED | No – the system does not transfer the conversation transcript to the human reviewer; only the handoff record and evidence can be accessed, without a live transcript. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 481 | Should the evidence package be transferred? | PARTIAL | The evidence package is only partially transferred: the handoff record contains the category, application reference and applicant’s message, and the reviewer can retrieve the decision evidence via the application reference and audit log, but there is no live UI transfer of the full evidence package. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 482 | Should the system explain why it escalated? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system explains why it escalated, using a template that states the reason (e.g., a reported data error, the system being unreachable, or manual review with its flags). | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 483 | Can the human agent continue from the same conversation state? | ANSWERED | No. The handoff creates a record but does not transfer the conversation; the human reviewer must start without the LLM's conversation state. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |

## 23. AGENTIC AI

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 484 | What exactly makes this system "agentic"? | PARTIAL | The system is agentic because it includes a single LangGraph‑based advisory agent that orchestrates tools (policy RAG, decision‑evidence retrieval, what‑if simulations) to generate grounded explanations, consuming only the decision evidence and never making its own underwriting decisions. | PR #6 (feat/langgraph-agent): Phase 5: single LangGraph advisory agent over ZEN evidence, Frappe and policy RAG (explains, never decides) § Please read: honest caveats; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Target later architecture; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § 5. Current technology decisions at a glance |
| 485 | What decisions should the agent make dynamically? | ANSWERED | The agent should not make any underwriting decisions; it only decides dynamically which explanatory action to take (e.g., explain, what‑if, improvement plan, citation, or abstain) based on the decision evidence and guard checks. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/__init__.py`; PR #6 (feat/langgraph-agent): Phase 5: single LangGraph advisory agent over ZEN evidence, Frappe and policy RAG (explains, never decides) § What is in it |
| 486 | Which tools should the agent be able to call? | ANSWERED | The agent is limited to a set of least‑privilege tools: `authorize`, `get_application_summary`, `get_decision_evidence`, `run_what_if`, `improvement_plan`, `search_approved_policy`, and `create_human_handoff`. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`); `docs/agent/README.md` § Tools (`tools.py`, the only things the graph can touch) |
| 487 | When should it retrieve applicant data? | ANSWERED | The agent retrieves applicant data only after the user is authenticated and the request is authorized for that applicant (e.g., an eligibility question, explanation, what‑if or policy lookup). If there is an identity or access failure, it must stop and not retrieve any application data. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Initial scope decisions |
| 488 | When should it retrieve policy? | ANSWERED | The agent should retrieve policy whenever the user asks a general policy question – i.e., when intent classification routes the request to the retrieve_policy node. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Proposed graph states/nodes |
| 489 | When should it calculate eligibility metrics? | PARTIAL | Eligibility metrics are calculated after the Frappe data is adapted and validated into the canonical schema, i.e., inside the ZEN decision engine before decision evidence is produced for the agent. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 490 | When should it run a counterfactual? | PARTIAL | The system runs a counterfactual only when the what‑if/improvement‑plan tool is explicitly invoked (e.g., in response to a user request), and it is limited to changing the four scored input fields. The simulation re‑evaluates the unchanged ZEN rules and returns a simulated result. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `docs/agent/README.md` § Tools (`tools.py`, the only things the graph can touch) |
| 491 | When should it check disclosure permissions? | ANSWERED | The system must verify disclosure permissions at every point it interacts with the borrower – i.e., at each stage of the interface before accessing or sharing any personal data. | policy source RBI-DLD-2025 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12848&Mode=0> |
| 492 | When should it request clarification? | ANSWERED | The agent should request clarification whenever it encounters missing or conflicting evidence (or ambiguous input), triggering a clarification step or escalation to a human. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |
| 493 | When should it refuse? | ANSWERED | The agent must refuse any request that matches a safety guard – e.g., asking for a guarantee of approval, to override or approve a decision, to misrepresent or forge information, to obtain confidential internal details, to inject prompts, to submit/re‑apply on the applicant’s behalf, or when the application is unknown/not authorized. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `tests/test_misrepresentation_guard.py`; `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 494 | When should it escalate? | ANSWERED | The agent escalates whenever it encounters missing, inconsistent, or disputed information in the decision evidence or applicant input, i.e., when it cannot reliably explain or resolve the issue. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § 1. Project objective and product definition; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries |
| 495 | Should the architecture use one agent or multiple agents? | ANSWERED | The architecture should use a single agent – a single LangGraph graph – and explicitly avoids a multi‑agent design. | memory: project note (loan-advisory-agent) § Loan Advisory Agent (VaaniLabs.AI capstone; repo Narendarcodes/Load-Advisory-Agent, "Load" spelling); run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks |
| 496 | What responsibilities would separate agents actually provide? | N/A | The project explicitly decides against using separate agents; all responsibilities (explanation, policy retrieval, what‑if analysis) are handled by a single LangGraph advisory agent, not split across multiple agents. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 5 --- LangGraph Loan Advisory Agent; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Initial scope decisions |
| 497 | Would multi-agent architecture add real value? | ANSWERED | The team decided that a multi‑agent architecture would not add real value for the MVP; they chose a single controlled LangGraph graph instead, citing added complexity, latency and evaluation burden. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 5 --- LangGraph Loan Advisory Agent; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks |
| 498 | What simpler architecture could solve the same problem? | ANSWERED | A simpler architecture is the conventional workflow that uses only Frappe Lending, the ZEN decision engine and deterministic template explanations, without any LLM or LangGraph layer. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Baselines (s.45) |
| 499 | What agent pattern is most suitable? | ANSWERED | The project selects a single‑agent pattern using a LangGraph advisory graph; a multi‑agent approach is explicitly rejected for the MVP. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Initial scope decisions; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Target later architecture; run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`) |
| 500 | What state should the agent maintain? | ANSWERED | The agent should only keep the conversation state (current intent, prior turns, clarification, tool outputs, thread/checkpointer), while the authoritative loan state stays in Frappe and Decision Evidence services. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 501 | What actions should the agent never be allowed to perform? | ANSWERED | The agent must never invent or modify any loan‑related facts (e.g., rejection reasons, thresholds, fees, policies, waiting periods, or eligibility outcomes), promise approval, alter the original application or decision, or treat retrieved RAG text as instructions. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries; `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `docs/architecture/repository-structure.md` § Boundaries |

## 24. MODEL VS CODE VS RETRIEVAL

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 502 | What should the LLM decide? | ANSWERED | The LLM must not make any underwriting decision; the decision (approve/reject) is produced solely by the ZEN deterministic engine, and the LLM’s role is limited to explaining the evidence. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries |
| 503 | What should deterministic code decide? | ANSWERED | Deterministic code must make the underwriting decision (approve or reject) by applying the ZEN rule engine and emit structured decision evidence; the LLM agent only explains the result. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 504 | What should database queries decide? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what database queries should decide; this design decision must be answered by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 505 | What should retrieval decide? | ANSWERED | Retrieval’s role is only to fetch relevant policy passages for citation; it never decides loan eligibility, which remains the responsibility of the ZEN decision engine. | `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § Phase 4: Policy source register and hybrid retrieval (EmbeddingGemma 2 + Qdrant + BM25) |
| 506 | What should policy rules decide? | ANSWERED | Policy rules should be the sole determinant of loan eligibility decisions, encoded in the ZEN decision models; retrieval and the agent only provide evidence, never decide eligibility. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § Phase 4: Policy source register and hybrid retrieval (EmbeddingGemma 2 + Qdrant + BM25) |
| 507 | What should a validation layer decide? | ANSWERED | The validation layer decides whether the mapped input passes the canonical pydantic schema; on failure it returns an ERROR evidence object instead of a fabricated decision. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `docs/prototypes/p0-integration.md` § P0 Integration; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py` |
| 508 | Which calculations must never be generated by the LLM? | ANSWERED | The LLM must never generate any underwriting calculations such as rejection reasons, thresholds, fees, policy parameters, waiting periods, or eligibility outcomes; these must come only from the deterministic ZEN engine. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent boundaries; `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 509 | Which outputs require deterministic validation? | ANSWERED | All outputs that leave the deterministic core must be validated: the canonical schema produced by the Frappe‑adapter mapping, the ZEN decision/evidence payload, and any LLM answer (including streamed sentences and citation grounding) are run through deterministic guards before being emitted. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/streaming.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/frappe_adapter.py` |
| 510 | Which outputs can be generated freely? | PARTIAL | The advisory agent can freely generate any of the five output types defined in the contract: answer, clarify, simulate, handoff, or refuse. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Output contract (proposal) |
| 511 | How do we verify an LLM answer against evidence? | ANSWERED | The system verifies an LLM answer by post‑hoc citation verification: in `verify` mode it keeps a cited passage only if at least one answer sentence is lexically supported by that passage, and optionally (`llm_verify` mode) an extra LLM call checks support, all without altering the answer generation. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/citations.py` |
| 512 | Should the LLM produce structured intermediate output? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the LLM should emit structured intermediate output; this design decision is not addressed. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs |  |
| 513 | Should the model first produce claims and then explanations? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify whether the model should first produce claims and then explanations. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 514 | Should each claim contain an evidence reference? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 515 | Should the system have a separate response validator? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether a separate response validator should be added; the decision is left to VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs |  |
| 516 | What should happen if the validator rejects an answer? | ANSWERED | If the validator rejects an answer, the system retries the LLM once and, if it still fails, falls back to a deterministic template answer, marking the response with a `fallback_template` guard flag. | `tests/test_agent_graph.py`; run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 3. Text-agent evaluation — `eval_v3/report.json` (verified) |
| 517 | Should the answer be regenerated? | N/A | The project’s current phase performs only retrieval and does not generate answers, so there is no answer to regenerate. | PR #4 (feat/policy-register-rag): Phase 4: policy register + hybrid RAG (EmbeddingGemma 2 + Qdrant + BM25) with 96-question eval § **Phase 4: policy source register + hybrid RAG evaluation** (stacked 4/4, on #3). Retrieval only; no answer generation, no agent.; `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § Phase 4: Policy source register and hybrid retrieval (EmbeddingGemma 2 + Qdrant + BM25) |
| 518 | Should the answer be blocked? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether a particular answer should be blocked; only that guard code blocks certain ungrounded content. Determination requires input from VaaniLabs or the project team. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 519 | Should it be sent to a human? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system is designed to hand off to a human whenever a data dispute, Frappe‑system unavailability, manual review flag, or an unsupported override request occurs. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing |

## 25. RAG / KNOWLEDGE REPRESENTATION

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 520 | What knowledge does the agent need? | ANSWERED | The agent must consume the structured Decision Evidence from the ZEN engine, retrieve the relevant policy knowledge from a controlled, versioned document store/retrieval index, and rely on the canonical loan state (not raw Frappe data). | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Current foundation |
| 521 | Which knowledge belongs in structured databases? | PARTIAL | The project stores operational loan data in Frappe Lending and the resulting decision evidence in a structured decision‑evidence layer, while policy knowledge is kept in a separate versioned document store. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Current foundation; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation |
| 522 | Which knowledge belongs in documents? | ANSWERED | Policy knowledge – such as RBI regulations, lender fair‑practice codes, product policies and other official guidance – belongs in the versioned document store and retrieval index, while underwriting rules and loan state remain in the ZEN decision models and Frappe system. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 523 | Which knowledge belongs in a rules engine? | ANSWERED | The rules engine should contain the underwriting rules – the policy logic, thresholds and decision criteria – and nothing else (no prompts, no Frappe logic, no agent logic). | `APPROACH.md` § Approach |
| 524 | Which knowledge belongs in a vector database? | ANSWERED | The vector database should store the policy knowledge – the controlled, versioned corpus of official policy, regulatory and lender documents (with metadata) – and must not contain applicant PII or underwriting rules. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § State/memory separation; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks |
| 525 | Which policy information should be retrieved semantically? | ANSWERED | The system should semantically retrieve policy chunks filtered by metadata such as jurisdiction, product, effective dates, policy version, approval status and document type. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 4 --- RAG implementation and evaluation; `rag/corpus.py` |
| 526 | Which policy information should be retrieved deterministically? | ANSWERED | The system must retrieve only policy documents that are in the approved (current) status, using the policy register and governance filter to enforce deterministic selection of approved policy information. | `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § 2. Retrieval design (`rag/`) |
| 527 | How should policy versions be stored? | ANSWERED | Policy versions are stored in a versioned registry (registry.json) that pins each policy‑JDM pair by SHA‑256, with separate JSON files for each version and metadata (status, effective dates, synthetic flag) retained for audit. | `decision_engine/policies/registry.json`; `decision_engine/policies/registry.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 4 --- RAG implementation and evaluation |
| 528 | How should reason-code definitions be stored? | ANSWERED | Reason‑code definitions are stored in the ZEN policy JSON (e.g. decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json) and are persisted with each rule evaluation in the decision‑evidence record (a custom Frappe DocType). | `decision_engine/policies/personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json`; `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; PR #3 (feat/frappe-zen-workflow): Phase 3: conventional Frappe+ZEN workflow, versioned policy, stored decision evidence § **Phase 3: conventional Frappe Lending + ZEN workflow, no LLM** (stacked 3/4, on #2) |
| 529 | How should applicant records be stored? | ANSWERED | Applicant records should be kept in the Frappe Lending system (the operational loan‑management database) with only the required Customer ID and name (optional email/phone) and never persisted in vector indexes or LLM traces. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Current foundation; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Architecture risks; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § A. Applicant identity/reference |
| 530 | How should decision records be stored? | ANSWERED | Decision records are persisted as a custom “Loan Decision Evidence” document in Frappe (via the FrappeEvidenceSink), with raw ZEN traces stored separately in a content‑addressed directory and referenced by SHA‑256. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/evidence_store.py`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`) |
| 531 | How should evidence links be stored? | PARTIAL | The materials note that Frappe does not have a field for evidence links, so links must be stored outside the Frappe loan record, e.g., as part of the Decision Evidence contract or in a separate evidence store. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 10. Decision storage; `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 532 | How do we prevent outdated policy documents from being retrieved? | ANSWERED | Outdated policies are filtered out by applying a governance filter before ranking that only serves documents with an ‘approved’ status (or approved_historical for historical mode) and excludes pending‑review or superseded versions. | `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § 2. Retrieval design (`rag/`); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 4 --- RAG implementation and evaluation; `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § 4. Results (droplet, 2026-10-14; `docs/policy/rag_eval_v1_report.json`) |
| 533 | How do we prevent irrelevant policies from being retrieved? | ANSWERED | Irrelevant policies are blocked by a governance filter that runs before any ranking: only chunks whose `approval_status` matches the requested mode (e.g., `approved` for current queries, `approved` + `approved_historical` for historical) are allowed, while pending‑review or non‑ingested documents are excluded, and lender‑profile filters further restrict by entity type and lender ID. | `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § 2. Retrieval design (`rag/`); `tests/test_bajaj_rag.py`; `rag/retrieve.py` |
| 534 | How do we verify retrieval correctness? | ANSWERED | Retrieval correctness is verified with the project’s evaluation harness: a 96‑question benchmark reports standard IR metrics (R@1, R@5, MRR@10, nDCG@10) and citation‑verification scripts compare retrieved passages against gold documents. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); PR #4 (feat/policy-register-rag): Phase 4: policy register + hybrid RAG (EmbeddingGemma 2 + Qdrant + BM25) with 96-question eval § **Phase 4: policy source register + hybrid RAG evaluation** (stacked 4/4, on #3). Retrieval only; no answer generation, no agent.; `scripts/policy/eval_bajaj_answers.py` |
| 535 | What happens when retrieval returns no result? | ANSWERED | If the policy retriever returns no passages, the graph returns an empty `passages` list (and metadata) – the turn proceeds with no cited text. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 536 | What happens when retrieval returns multiple conflicting results? | ANSWERED | When retrieval returns multiple conflicting results, the system surfaces the conflict rather than arbitrarily choosing one side, leaving the conflict visible for downstream handling. | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources); `docs/policy/policy_register_and_rag.md` § 2. Retrieval design (`rag/`) |

## 26. DECISION TRACEABILITY

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 537 | Can every answer be traced back to its source? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system records decision evidence that includes provenance and trace references for every rule outcome, so each answer can be linked back to its source. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Current foundation; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 2 --- data pipeline, conventional automation and decision engine |
| 538 | Can every claim be traced to applicant data, decision data, or policy? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system is designed so that every claim in the agent’s response is backed by either applicant data, decision evidence, or policy, enforced by deterministic guards and explicit citations to Decision Evidence records. | `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`); `infra/frappe/bootstrap/seed_site.py`; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 539 | Should the user be able to inspect that trace? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether end‑users are allowed to view the ZEN trace; they only provide developer tools to inspect it. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 540 | What level of transparency should be shown to the applicant? | ANSWERED | The applicant should see only their own decision, the rule checks that led to it, and the values they submitted; internal details such as who handled the file or internal grades/scores must not be shown. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 541 | What level of transparency should be shown to a support agent? | ANSWERED | Support agents should be shown the full normalized decision evidence – rule‑level outcomes, reasons, provenance and trace references – accessed via the least‑privilege `get_decision_evidence` tool, without exposing raw Frappe data. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Current foundation; run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`) |
| 542 | What should be logged internally? | ANSWERED | The orchestrator records a log entry containing the UTC timestamp, application_id, the decision outcome, the count of rule evaluations, the execution time in milliseconds, a flag indicating if a ZEN trace is available, and any error message. When tracing is enabled, span logs also capture ids, intents, flags, token counts and timings. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 543 | What should not be logged? | ANSWERED | Raw message and answer text must not be exported in traces by default. Two independent, off-by-default gates control content: `AGENT_TRACE_CONTENT=1` for the Phoenix exporter and `LANGFUSE_CAPTURE_CONTENT=1` for the optional Langfuse exporter; enabling one does not enable the other. Without them, spans carry IDs, intents, flags, token counts and timings only. The Langfuse processor also deletes `input.value`, `output.value`, `llm.system` and `llm.error` at export. The audit log and handoff queue are separate from tracing and do store message text. | `docs/observability/langfuse.md` § Privacy boundary; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/observability.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/langfuse_config.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 544 | Should the system produce an evidence graph? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify whether the system should produce an evidence graph; this decision must be clarified by the project team. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 545 | Should each response have a machine-readable explanation record? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 546 | Can a reviewer reproduce why the agent gave a particular answer? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system records a full decision evidence package—including rule evaluations, a ZEN execution trace, and an audit log—so a reviewer can reopen the evidence by application reference and see exactly why the answer was given. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py`; `docs/prototypes/p0-integration.md` § P0 Integration |
| 547 | Can we audit a conversation after the fact? | ANSWERED | Yes – the agent writes an audit row for every turn (ids, intent, flags, timings, etc.) to a JSONL file, which can be inspected later to trace the conversation. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 548 | Can we reproduce the exact policy version used? | ANSWERED | Yes – the exact policy version can be reproduced because the policy is loaded from a versioned registry that pins the policy file and JDM by SHA‑256 and records the policy id, version, and hashes in each Decision Evidence record. | `decision_engine/policies/registry.py`; `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`) |
| 549 | Can we reproduce the exact applicant data used? | PARTIAL | The system records the deterministic inputs (the four scored fields) and a hash of them, but it does not store full applicant PII, so only the decision‑relevant data can be reproduced, not the complete original applicant record. | `docs/architecture/conventional-workflow.md` § Decision Evidence record (`decision-evidence/1`); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 550 | Can we reproduce the exact prompt/model configuration used? | PARTIAL | The repository contains the model configuration (model name, temperature, max_tokens, reasoning effort, and RAG settings) in the LLM client code and rag.yaml, but the exact prompt text (system and user messages) is not included, so the full prompt cannot be reproduced from the provided materials. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/llm.py`; `config/rag.yaml` |

## 27. EXPLANATION QUALITY

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 551 | What makes a loan rejection explanation good? | ANSWERED | A good loan‑rejection explanation is specific, lists every failed underwriting reason with its value, is grounded in the decision evidence without hallucinated claims, uses clear language (low jargon) and, where possible, offers actionable steps, all while respecting the synthetic policy labeling. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B) |
| 552 | What makes it bad? | PARTIAL | A bad explanation is one that fails to cover all required reason‑value pairs, includes unsupported or hallucinated numbers, or violates deterministic guarantees – i.e., it is incomplete, ungrounded, or non‑deterministic. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B) |
| 553 | What makes an explanation understandable? | PARTIAL | An explanation is understandable when it is written in language the borrower can comprehend, using simple, readable wording and minimal jargon, and can be adapted to the applicant’s literacy level. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); policy source RBI-RBC-CB-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/170MD.PDF> |
| 554 | What makes an explanation actionable? | ANSWERED | An explanation is actionable when it is specific to the factors actually considered and tells the applicant what concrete change would lead to a favorable outcome—e.g., a counterfactual that shows the smallest change needed or a reason code that pinpoints the principal reason and suggests steps to improve credit status. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Counterfactual explanations; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 3. Published metric families for the evaluation questions (s.44, s.45) |
| 555 | What makes an explanation faithful? | ANSWERED | An explanation is faithful when it is directly derived from the underlying decision evidence and policy rules, accurately reflects the reasons and values used by the ZEN engine, and any claims are supported by citations that match the facts (i.e., no hallucinations). | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 3. Published metric families for the evaluation questions (s.44, s.45) |
| 556 | What makes an explanation complete? | PARTIAL | An explanation is complete when it includes every failed ZEN reason and the corresponding value in the explanation. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44) |
| 557 | What makes an explanation too technical? | ANSWERED | An explanation is considered too technical when it contains domain‑specific jargon or abbreviations (e.g., FOIR, DTI, ratio, threshold, tenure, bureau) or uses complex numeric expressions instead of simple everyday language, violating the low‑literacy style guidelines. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `scripts/agent/eval_agent.py` |
| 558 | What makes an explanation too vague? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not define criteria for when an explanation is considered too vague; the project team (or VaaniLabs) would need to specify this. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 559 | How many rejection reasons should be explained at once? | PARTIAL | The RBI Fair Practices Code requires lenders to convey the main reason(s) for a rejection, but it does not specify a fixed number, so the system can explain all identified main reasons. | policy source RBI-FPC-2007 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_CircularIndexDisplay.aspx?Id=3315> |
| 560 | Should the agent start with the main reason? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify whether the agent should begin its explanation with the main reason; they only require that all failed reasons be included. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 561 | Should it explain every failed criterion? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design mandates that for a REJECTED decision the agent must state every failed rule as a reason, including the applicant’s value and the required value. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 562 | Should it explain passed criteria as well? | ANSWERED | No, the system is designed not to explain passed criteria; explanations only list failed rule reasons. | `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 563 | Should it explicitly distinguish contributing factors from decisive factors? | PARTIAL | Regulatory guidance requires that reasons be specific and indicate the principal reason(s) for the adverse action, which implies a distinction between decisive and merely contributing factors, but the project’s design simply lists all failed rules as reasons without separating them. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B); `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 564 | Should it show numerical values? | ANSWERED | Yes – explanations must include the numerical values for each failed ZEN reason (but not for passed rules). | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44) |
| 565 | Should it show policy thresholds? | ANSWERED | Yes. The system is built to show the policy thresholds – the four synthetic thresholds – in explanations and decision cards, always labeled as the synthetic prototype policy. | `apps/web/chat/index.html`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/agent/README.md` § Phase 5 — Loan Advisory Agent (one LangGraph graph) + Phase 6 voice; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § P0 --- decision core |
| 566 | Should it show formulas? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether explanations should include formulas; the decision is left to the project team. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 567 | Should it show evidence sources? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design mandates that explanations include the decision evidence and the relevant policy, i.e., the source of the rule that led to the decision. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; `rag/corpus.py` |
| 568 | How should uncertainty be communicated? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how uncertainty should be communicated; the project team must define this. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 569 | How should limitations be communicated? | PARTIAL | Limitations should be communicated by explicitly stating that regulatory rules and company policies are distinguished and by noting the relevant policy version (e.g., synthetic vs historical) when giving an explanation. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Policy limitations |
| 570 | How should the agent explain that future approval cannot be guaranteed? | ANSWERED | The agent should invoke the `guarantee` guard and reply with a template such as “I can't promise or predict approval …” (and optionally provide an improvement plan), abstaining from guaranteeing future approval. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |

P1 requirements; policy source BFL-PL-OVERVIEW (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan>; policy source BFL-PL-RATES-FEES (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan-processing-fees-and-interest-rates> |

| 573 | Should it explain interest? | ANSWERED | Yes. Data correction and disputes (s.19); `tests/test_agent_upgrades.py`; `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 576 | Should it explain credit utilization? | ANSWERED | Yes, the advisory agent should explain credit utilization, as the project’s financial‑education guidance explicitly advises applicants to keep utilization below 30% of their credit limit. | policy source BFL-INSIGHT-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/insights/4-reasons-for-personal-loan-rejection-and-how-to-avoid-them> |
| 577 | Should it explain DTI? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 578 | Should it explain repayment history? | ANSWERED | Yes – the design treats repayment history as agent context for explanation and re‑application guidance, so the advisory agent should explain it. | `docs/research/frappe-requirements.md` § 11. Historical data |
| 579 | Should it explain employment tenure? | ANSWERED | Yes – the advisory agent’s explanation includes employment tenure, showing it as a failed rule when the applicant’s tenure is below 12 months. | `data/evaluation/rag/synthetic_docs/SYN-POLICY-V1.md` § Rule MIN_EMPLOYMENT_TENURE (reason code ERR_EMPLOYMENT_TENURE_SHORT); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § P0 --- decision core; `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |

| 582 | Should it explain eligibility criteria? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not state whether the advisory agent should explain eligibility criteria; this decision must be provided by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |

It will do so for banks (which must provide written reasons) and, for NBFCs, it will not claim a written‑reason requirement. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. | 584 | How much financial education is appropriate during a rejection conversation? | PARTIAL | The project cites RBI guidance that banks must disclose “full‑fledged information on its products and their implications” (P00548), suggesting the agent should provide comprehensive financial education, but it does not specify a concrete amount or limit for a rejection conversation. | policy source RBI-RBC-CB-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/170MD.PDF> |

| 593 | What signals should trigger more detailed explanations? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |

| 600 | How should it handle an applicant who wants only the key points? | PARTIAL | The design includes a “simple” branch in the LangGraph state machine that can be used to give a concise, key‑point summary, but the materials do not detail exactly how or when this is invoked for a user request for only key points. | run report: out/loan-agent/RESULTS.md § 2. Architecture (from `docs/agent/README.md`); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 601 | How can we evaluate adaptive education objectively? | ANSWERED | We can evaluate adaptive education objectively with the project's evaluation harness, which computes metrics such as communication‑adaptation (readability/jargon difference plus judge rating) alongside explanation completeness, hallucination rate, guarantee‑violation, abstention, what‑if correctness, reliability, latency and retrieval quality. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6.
