## 30. EDGE CASES — APPLICATION STATE

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 603 | What happens when the application does not exist? | ANSWERED | If the application reference is not found, the store.get call raises an UnknownApplication exception, which propagates out of process_application (and decide returns error evidence). No lifecycle transition occurs and no evidence is persisted. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py`; `tests/test_p3_workflow.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/frappe_adapter.py` |
| 604 | What happens when the application ID is invalid? | ANSWERED | An invalid application ID triggers a UnknownApplication exception, which is caught and results in error evidence with decision "ERROR" and an error message; no lifecycle state change occurs. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/infrastructure/frappe_adapter.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py` |
| 605 | What happens when the application belongs to another person? | ANSWERED | The system refuses to provide any information and returns a guard response stating it can only discuss the caller’s own application and cannot look up or share another person’s details. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 606 | What happens when there are multiple applications? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how the system behaves when multiple applications are present; clarification from the project team is required. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 607 | What happens when one application is approved and another is rejected? | PARTIAL | Each loan application is handled independently: an approved application ends up in the APPROVED state (status "Approved") and a rejected one in the REJECTED state (status "Rejected"), with separate evidence records persisted for each. | `tests/test_p3_workflow.py` |
| 608 | What happens when the application is pending? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 609 | What happens when the application is cancelled? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify the system behavior when an application is cancelled; this detail must be provided by the project team or the lender. **Who must answer:** a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 610 | What happens when the rejection has no recorded reason? | PARTIAL | Rejection reasons are taken directly from the failed rule evaluations recorded at decision time. If a rejection has no recorded failed rule, the system has no reason codes to report, so the agent would have nothing to list as a reason. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 611 | What happens when the rejection has multiple reasons? | PARTIAL | The prototype is built to record all rejection reasons and the agent is intended to explain each one, with test scenarios covering both single and multiple reasons, but the materials do not detail exactly how multiple reasons are presented to the applicant. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Agent evaluation targets (proposed, not achieved) |
| 612 | What happens when the rejection reason is contradictory? | ANSWERED | If the rejection reason is contradictory (e.g., obligations exceed income), the system flags CONTRADICTION_OBLIGATIONS_EXCEED_INCOME and routes the application to MANUAL_REVIEW for human handling. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py`; `tests/test_p3_workflow.py` |
| 613 | What happens when the application data is incomplete? | ANSWERED | If required fields are missing, the orchestrator creates an ERROR decision, records the missing fields, and transitions the application to the INFO_REQUESTED state. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py`; `tests/test_p3_workflow.py` |
| 614 | What happens when data is missing? | ANSWERED | For the ZEN decision path, a missing required input produces an ERROR decision, sets lifecycle to INFO_REQUESTED, records the missing fields and returns the application for more information rather than approving or rejecting it. The synthetic bureau service has distinct outcomes: a data-quality hold produces `outcome=REVIEW` (the legacy `decision` field stays `ERROR`) and moves the application to MANUAL_REVIEW (or INFO_REQUESTED when data is missing/invalid), while a provider failure such as HTTP 429/503 or a timeout produces `outcome=ERROR` with `retryable` set when a retry can help. It is a development-only stand-in, not CIBIL or a real credit bureau. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/bureau_intake.py`; `docs/architecture/synthetic-bureau.md` § REVIEW vs ERROR |
| 615 | What happens when data is outdated? | ANSWERED | If an applicant reports that their data is outdated, the system treats it as a dispute, creates a data‑dispute handoff to a human reviewer, and replies that it cannot edit the record itself. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 616 | What happens when data changes after rejection? | ANSWERED | The system does not automatically re‑evaluate a rejected application when its data changes; a decided record cannot be silently re‑decided and will raise an IllegalTransition unless the application is resubmitted, at which point a new decision (or the same evidence if data is unchanged) is generated. | `tests/test_p3_live.py` |

## 31. EDGE CASES — POLICY

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 617 | What happens when the policy is unavailable? | ANSWERED | If the policy cannot be retrieved, the agent abstains from answering and uses the unavailable/not‑available template, routing the turn to a handoff. | `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 618 | What happens when the policy is outdated? | PARTIAL | If the policy is outdated, the agent fails safely by abstaining from answering and returns no citations. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Phase 4 --- RAG implementation and evaluation; `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 619 | What happens when multiple policy versions exist? | ANSWERED | If a policy has several registered versions, the system stores them in the registry and load_policy() selects the version marked as active by default; a caller can also request a specific version, and an unknown version triggers an error. | `decision_engine/policies/registry.py`; `decision_engine/policies/registry.json` |
| 620 | What happens when the correct policy version cannot be identified? | ANSWERED | If the system cannot identify the correct policy version, load_policy raises an UnknownPolicyVersion exception, causing a fail‑closed error and preventing the policy from being loaded. | `decision_engine/policies/registry.py` |
| 621 | What happens when two policies conflict? | ANSWERED | When two policies conflict, the system registers the conflict and surfaces it to the user rather than arbitrarily selecting one policy. | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources) |
| 622 | What happens when the policy does not cover the applicant's question? | ANSWERED | If the policy does not cover the applicant's question, the classifier routes the query to the policy path (a cite‑or‑abstain path). The policy node then abstains, returning a generic fallback answer such as “Not in the sources.” or a localized “service is busy, ask again in a moment” template. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`; PR #8 (feat/agent-upgrades): Agent upgrades: guarded sentence streaming for voice, per-turn deadline + fallback, citation verification (opt-in), /voice contrast § What changes; `tests/test_agent_upgrades.py` |
| 623 | What happens when the policy contains an exception? | PARTIAL | The policy can include exception language (e.g., “the bank may consider such cases on merits and make an exception”), but the project materials do not describe any special system behavior for such exceptions – they are treated as part of the policy rules that the ZEN engine evaluates like any other clause. | policy source RBI-RBC-CB-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/170MD.PDF>; `decision_engine/policies/registry.py` |
| 624 | What happens when an exception requires human judgment? | ANSWERED | The system creates a handoff record and replies with action="handoff" (including a handoff reference), informing the applicant that a human reviewer will handle the exception. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 625 | What happens when a policy threshold is ambiguous? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how the system handles an ambiguous policy threshold; clarification from VaaniLabs or the project team is required. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 626 | What happens when the policy uses undefined terminology? | ANSWERED | If a policy contains undefined terminology, the ZEN engine will raise an error, which the orchestrator catches and returns an ERROR evidence object instead of a fabricated decision. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/decision_orchestrator.py` |
| 627 | What happens when the applicant asks for information that the policy does not disclose? | ANSWERED | The agent uses the `not_available` guard and replies that the requested information isn’t available, refusing to disclose it. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 628 | What happens when applicant data conflicts with decision data? | ANSWERED | When applicant data conflicts with decision data, the system creates a human handoff record and informs the applicant that their query has been escalated. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 629 | What happens when the evidence supporting a rejection reason is missing? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 630 | What happens when evidence is incomplete? | ANSWERED | If the decision evidence is incomplete, the system flags the missing or conflicting evidence and routes the request for clarification or human escalation, marking unevaluable rules as NOT_EVALUABLE in the evidence record. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 631 | What happens when evidence cannot be disclosed? | ANSWERED | When evidence cannot be disclosed, the agent uses the `not_available` guard and replies “That information isn’t available to me …”, abstaining from providing any citation. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 632 | What happens when the applicant disputes the evidence? | ANSWERED | When an applicant disputes the evidence, the system routes the request to the boundary path, creates a data‑dispute handoff record and replies with a template stating the issue has been passed to a human reviewer, including a reference ID; the response carries `action="handoff"`. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 633 | What happens when multiple data sources disagree? | ANSWERED | When sources conflict the system records both values, surfaces the disagreement (e.g., test LB‑024 shows the conflict) and routes the case to MANUAL_REVIEW rather than arbitrarily picking one. | `docs/policy/bajaj_reference_lender.md` § 2.3 Conflict and staleness register (Bajaj sources); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 634 | What happens when the system cannot establish which source was used? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what the system does when it cannot determine which source was used; this behavior is not documented. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |

## 33. EDGE CASES — CONVERSATION

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 635 | What happens when the applicant changes topics? | PARTIAL | When the applicant shifts to a new, unrelated subject, the classifier tags the message as the “other” label (off‑topic) and the system routes the turn to the generic off‑topic response path. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 636 | What happens when the applicant asks a question unrelated to loans? | ANSWERED | The agent classifies the off‑topic message as intent "other" and routes it to the simple node, which replies with a generic fallback template and does not provide loan advice. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 637 | What happens when the applicant repeats the same question? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify the system's behavior when an applicant repeats the same question. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 638 | What happens when the applicant asks a question with ambiguous meaning? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify how the agent handles a question with ambiguous meaning. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 639 | What happens when the applicant contradicts something they said earlier? | ANSWERED | When a contradiction is detected (e.g., obligations exceed income), the system adds a CONTRADICTION flag and routes the application to MANUAL_REVIEW rather than auto‑approving or rejecting. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py` |
| 640 | What happens when the applicant provides new information? | ANSWERED | If the applicant provides new information that indicates a possible error (e.g., wrong salary or credit details), the system treats it as a data dispute, routes the turn to the boundary path, creates a handoff record, and replies that a human reviewer will check the information, noting it cannot edit the record itself. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19) |
| 641 | What happens when the applicant asks a hypothetical question? | ANSWERED | The system classifies the query as a "what_if" intent, validates that only the four allowed numeric fields are changed, runs a deterministic ZEN simulation, and returns a response describing the simulated outcome while stating it is not a decision or guarantee and noting any limitations. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py`; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Routing; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/application/lifecycle.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 642 | What happens when the applicant asks multiple questions in one message? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not describe what the agent does when an applicant includes multiple questions in a single message, so the behavior is undefined. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 643 | What happens when the applicant sends incomplete messages? | ANSWERED | When the applicant sends an incomplete message the system’s guard detects it and returns the “missing‑info” template, prompting the user for the required details instead of a decision. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `docs/agent/README.md` § Graph (`apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py`) |
| 644 | What happens when the applicant switches between English and Telugu? | PARTIAL | The system supports English, Hindi and Telugu via locale settings, but multilingual coverage is limited; switching languages mid‑conversation is not fully handled and may fall back to the known‑limitation guard. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 645 | What happens when the applicant uses slang? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not describe any specific handling for slang used by an applicant; the behavior is not defined. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 646 | What happens when the applicant makes spelling or grammar mistakes? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how the system handles applicant spelling or grammar mistakes; the team must define this behavior. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 647 | What happens when the applicant uses financial terminology incorrectly? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify how the system responds when an applicant uses financial terminology incorrectly. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 648 | What happens when the applicant is angry? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify any special handling for an angry applicant; the team or VaaniLabs would need to define the behavior. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 649 | What happens when the applicant requests a guarantee? | ANSWERED | When the applicant asks for a guarantee, the request is caught by the guarantee guard, the agent refuses and abstains from promising approval, returns a template saying it cannot guarantee approval, and if the loan was rejected it also attaches the deterministic improvement plan. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` |
| 650 | What happens when the applicant asks the agent to "just approve" the loan? | ANSWERED | The request triggers the `override` guard and the agent refuses, replying with a template such as “I can't approve, reverse or change a loan decision …”. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |

## 34. EDGE CASES — COUNTERFACTUALS

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 651 | What happens when the hypothetical change is impossible? | ANSWERED | If a requested hypothetical change is impossible (outside the allowed fields or numeric limits), the system rejects it with a `bad_what_if` response that clarifies the allowed fields. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 652 | What happens when the hypothetical change is outside the policy? | ANSWERED | If a hypothetical change is outside the allowed fields or numeric limits, the system rejects it with a `bad_what_if` status, raising an InvalidWhatIf error and returning a clarification that only the four scored fields within their ranges are allowed; no simulation is performed. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py` |
| 653 | What happens when the hypothetical changes multiple criteria? | ANSWERED | The system runs a simulation with all provided changes together, returning a combined hypothetical decision labeled as a simulation and not a guarantee of approval. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 5. What-if / counterfactual representation (s.12) |
| 654 | What happens when a hypothetical satisfies one rule but fails another? | ANSWERED | The what‑if tool re‑runs ZEN with the changed input, marks the modified rule as passed, but any other rules that still fail remain FAIL, so the overall decision stays REJECTED. | `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Labels and counterfactuals; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py` |
| 655 | What happens when the hypothetical depends on unknown future information? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify how the system handles a hypothetical that relies on unknown future information; the current counterfactuals are deterministic single‑field changes with no provision for future‑unknown data. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 656 | What happens when the model cannot calculate the hypothetical reliably? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what the system does when a hypothetical cannot be calculated reliably. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 657 | What happens when the applicant asks for the exact combination of values required for approval? | ANSWERED | If the applicant provides the exact combination of values that meet the rule thresholds, the ZEN engine will return an APPROVED decision, as the edge‑case tests confirm that exact‑boundary inputs are approved. | `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Coverage (measured) |
| 658 | What happens when the applicant tries to reverse-engineer the underwriting criteria? | ANSWERED | The agent will refuse to aid reverse‑engineering, invoking the `misrepresentation` guard and responding that it cannot help hide, inflate, edit or fake information and that such attempts may be treated as fraud. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 659 | What happens when the applicant asks how to manipulate their application? | ANSWERED | The system flags such requests as a misrepresentation request and returns a deterministic refusal, refusing to help and warning that the behavior may be treated as fraud. | `tests/test_misrepresentation_guard.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |

## 35. EDGE CASES — SAFETY / COMPLIANCE

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 660 | What happens when the applicant asks for confidential internal information? | ANSWERED | When an applicant requests confidential internal information (e.g., who decided or internal grade/score), the system triggers the `not_available` guard and replies with “That information isn’t available to me …”, abstaining from providing the data. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 661 | What happens when the applicant asks for another person's data? | ANSWERED | If an applicant asks for another person's data, the system’s `other_applicant` guard blocks the request and replies it can only discuss the caller’s own application, never sharing or looking up anyone else’s details. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 662 | What happens when the applicant asks for hidden model details? | ANSWERED | The agent refuses and abstains, using the `not_available` guard to reply that the hidden model details aren’t available to it. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 663 | What happens when the applicant asks for internal risk signals? | ANSWERED | The agent will refuse to disclose internal risk signals and reply with an honest “that isn’t available to me” response. | `tests/test_abstain_unavailable.py` |
| 664 | What happens when the applicant asks for a guaranteed approval? | ANSWERED | When an applicant asks for a guaranteed approval, the system activates the `guarantee` request guard, refuses to promise or predict approval, may provide an improvement plan if the application was rejected, and otherwise abstains. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` |
| 665 | What happens when the applicant asks for a method to bypass eligibility? | ANSWERED | The agent treats a request to bypass eligibility as a misrepresentation request and refuses, replying with a message like “I can’t help hide, inflate, edit or fake any information … may be treated as fraud.” | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 666 | What happens when the applicant appears to be committing fraud? | ANSWERED | If the applicant appears to be committing fraud, the system triggers the `misrepresentation` guard and refuses to assist, responding that it cannot help hide, inflate, edit or fake information and that the behavior may be treated as fraud; it does not report the suspected fraud. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 667 | What happens when the applicant requests help falsifying information? | ANSWERED | The agent flags the request as a misrepresentation, refuses to comply, and replies with the misrepresentation template stating it cannot help hide, inflate, edit or fake any information and that false information may be treated as fraud. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35); `tests/test_misrepresentation_guard.py`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` |
| 668 | What happens when the applicant asks the agent to change the application data? | ANSWERED | The agent cannot modify any application data; it refuses the request with a fixed message and offers to run a what‑if simulation or forward the correction to a human reviewer. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 1. Data correction and disputes (s.19); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |
| 669 | What happens when the applicant attempts prompt injection? | ANSWERED | The system detects the prompt-injection attempt and routes the request to the injection guard, replying with a deterministic template that says the rules don't change based on any message instructions. The guard is a set of deterministic patterns in `guards.py` ('ignore previous instructions', 'system prompt', 'developer mode', 'jailbreak', 'reveal your prompt' and similar); a hit takes the boundary path before the model can act on it. Coverage is pattern-based English plus a few Hindi/Telugu terms, so it is not a complete defence against every paraphrase. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/guards.py` § `_REQ["injection"]`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/graph.py` § `boundary`; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/prompts.py` § `TEMPLATES["en"]["injection"]` |
| 670 | What happens when retrieved documents contain malicious instructions? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not describe how the system handles retrieved documents that contain malicious instructions; the project team must define this behavior. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 671 | What happens when an external tool returns unexpected information? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not describe how the system handles unexpected information from an external tool. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |

## 38. EXISTING SOLUTIONS

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 701 | What products already explain loan rejection reasons? | PARTIAL | The materials show that Bajaj Finance – a public reference NBFC – provides a product that informs applicants of rejection reasons via an SMS and publishes an insights article listing common rejection factors, but no other existing products are described. | policy source BFL-FAQ-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/contact-us-faq-queries>; policy source BFL-INSIGHT-REJECTION (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/insights/4-reasons-for-personal-loan-rejection-and-how-to-avoid-them> |
| 702 | What products already provide loan eligibility guidance? | ANSWERED | Existing products that already provide loan‑eligibility guidance include the Personal Loan Eligibility Calculators and online eligibility‑check tools offered by ICICI Bank, HDFC Bank and Bajaj Finance (e.g., mobile‑OTP eligibility check and pre‑approved loan offers). | policy source ICICI-PL-ELIG (approved) <https://www.icicibank.com/personal-banking/loans/personal-loan/eligibility>; policy source BFL-PL-ELIG-DOCS (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan-eligibility-and-documents> |
| 703 | What products already provide financial education? | ANSWERED | The materials show that the personal‑loan product already provides financial‑education content (e.g., Bajaj Finance’s personal‑loan education article). | `data/sources/policy_register.yaml`; policy source BFL-PL-OVERVIEW (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/personal-loan> |
| 704 | What products already provide AI-based financial guidance? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 705 | What products already provide personalized loan recommendations? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not list any existing products that already provide personalized loan recommendations. **Who must answer:** a partner lender / the project team |  |
| 706 | What products already answer loan-support questions? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not list any existing products that already answer loan‑support questions; they note that a static FAQ or human‑support baseline was not run. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 707 | What products already use conversational AI in lending? | UNKNOWN | The provided project materials do not list any existing lending products that use conversational AI. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 708 | What products already use agentic AI in financial services? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not list any existing financial‑service products that use agentic AI. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 709 | What products already perform counterfactual credit explanations? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not list any existing products that perform counterfactual credit explanations; this information must be supplied by the project team or VaaniLabs. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 710 | What products already use explainable AI for credit decisions? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not list any existing products that use explainable AI for credit decisions; this information must be supplied by external sources such as lenders or market researchers. **Who must answer:** a partner lender |  |
| 711 | What products already provide human handoff from AI support? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not list any existing products that already provide human handoff from AI support. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 712 | What capabilities do these products provide? | UNKNOWN | The project materials do not describe the capabilities of existing loan‑advisory products; only the project's own prototype capabilities are detailed. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 713 | What data do they use? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify what data existing solutions use; they only describe the project's own data sources (LendingClub, UCI German Credit, synthetic personas). **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 714 | What models do they use? | ANSWERED | They use a deterministic ZEN decision engine, a hybrid retriever that combines dense embeddings from the Google EmbeddingGemma‑2 model (via Qdrant) with BM25, a cross‑encoder reranker BAAI/bge‑reranker‑v2‑m3, and a 120 B‑parameter LLM (gpt‑oss‑120b) served via vLLM. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/runtime.py`; `scripts/data/normalise_datasets.py` |
| 715 | How do they retrieve policy information? | ANSWERED | They retrieve policy information via a hybrid RAG pipeline: policy documents are chunked, embedded with EmbeddingGemma 2 and indexed in Qdrant, then searched with dense vector similarity and BM25, filtered by lender profile, and fused (weighted RRF) with optional reranking. | `docs/agent/README.md` § How it queries the policy RAG; `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py` |
| 716 | How do they handle privacy? | ANSWERED | Bajaj Finance handles privacy by tracking personal data across all assets, securing it with encryption and masking, applying strict access controls, and using DLP and SOC tools to prevent leakage, while also offering data‑erasure requests and complying with retention guidelines. | policy source BFL-PRIVACY (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/privacy-policy> |
| 717 | How do they handle compliance? | PARTIAL | The prototype enforces compliance by mapping Frappe records to a canonical Pydantic schema and rejecting any invalid data with error evidence, and by emitting structured decision evidence for downstream use. It also cites external compliance policies (e.g., Bajaj Finance KYC & AML policy, RBI reporting directions), but those regulatory processes are not implemented in the current prototype. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach; policy source BFL-KYC-PMLA-POLICY (pending_review) <https://cms-assets.bajajfinserv.in/is/content/bajajfinancestage/kyc-and-pmla-policypdf?fmt=pdf&scl=1> |
| 718 | How do they evaluate their systems? | ANSWERED | The project evaluates its systems through automated tests (pytest smoke and integration tests) and a dedicated evaluation harness that measures metrics such as explanation completeness, hallucination rate, citation support, latency, and cost proxies for both the ZEN engine and the policy‑RAG/agent components. | `tests/test_zen_smoke.py`; `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `scripts/agent/eval_agent.py` |
| 719 | What limitations do existing solutions have? | ANSWERED | Existing solutions are limited by the fact that public company pages often do not disclose internal underwriting rules and public data may lack authentic Indian applicant decisions and rejection reasons, making policy retrieval and explanation incomplete. | handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Policy limitations; handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § Data limitations |
| 720 | What problem do existing solutions fail to solve? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 721 | What part of our proposed system would genuinely be different? | PARTIAL | The proposal’s novel element is the separation of a deterministic ZEN underwriting engine and a decision‑evidence layer from the LLM‑based advisory agent, rather than embedding rules in prompts or the agent. | `APPROACH.md` § Approach |

## 39. EXPLAINABLE AI / XAI

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 722 | How are credit decisions currently explained? | ANSWERED | Credit decisions are currently explained via specific adverse‑action reason codes that list the principal factors considered, as required by regulations, rather than generic internal policy statements. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B) |
| 723 | What are reason codes? | ANSWERED | Reason codes are the identifiers attached to each rule evaluation that indicate why a rule failed; they are collected as `failed_reason_codes` and included in the decision evidence shown to the applicant. | `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/facts.py`; `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py`; `scripts/data/generate_personas.py` |
| 724 | How are reason codes generated? | ANSWERED | Reason codes are defined in the policy JSON for each rule and are looked up when building decision evidence; the engine inserts the rule's `reason_code` into the evidence and record. | `decision_engine/evidence/decision_evidence.py`; `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 725 | What is SHAP? | ANSWERED | SHAP (SHapley Additive exPlanations) is a unified framework for interpreting predictions that assigns each feature an importance value for a particular prediction. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § SHAP |
| 726 | What is LIME? | ANSWERED | LIME is a technique that explains the predictions of any classifier in an interpretable and faithful way by learning a simple, interpretable model locally around each prediction. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § LIME |
| 727 | What are counterfactual explanations? | ANSWERED | Counterfactual explanations describe the minimal change to the input that would lead to a desired (e.g., approved) outcome, without requiring an explanation of the system’s internal logic. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Counterfactual explanations |
| 728 | Which explanation methods are used in lending? | ANSWERED | The project notes that lending explanations can use SHAP, LIME, counterfactual explanations, and rule‑based reason codes (failed rules) as the concrete reasons recorded at decision time. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § SHAP; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § LIME; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Counterfactual explanations; `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 729 | Which methods are appropriate for rule-based decisions? | ANSWERED | For rule‑based decisions the appropriate XAI method is to directly report the recorded failed rules (deterministic explanation) and use counterfactual what‑if re‑runs, without any post‑hoc approximations such as SHAP or LIME. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `tests/test_agent_graph.py` |
| 730 | Which methods are appropriate for ML-based decisions? | ANSWERED | Post‑hoc explanation methods such as SHAP, LIME or similar approximations are appropriate for ML‑based decisions, but they must be validated by the lender before use. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B); `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 731 | What is the difference between feature importance and causal explanation? | PARTIAL | Feature‑importance methods (e.g., SHAP) assign each input a numeric contribution to a particular prediction, whereas a causal explanation would describe how changing a feature would actually cause the outcome to change – the project materials define the former but do not provide a definition of the latter. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § SHAP |
| 732 | Can a model explanation accurately represent why a loan was rejected? | ANSWERED | Yes – the system records the exact ZEN rules that caused the rejection and the agent only reports those recorded failed rules, so the explanation faithfully reflects why the loan was rejected. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § ZEN Engine behavior discovered |
| 733 | What are the limitations of post-hoc explanations? | ANSWERED | Post‑hoc explanations are only approximations of the underlying model and may not accurately reflect the true decision logic; therefore they must be validated for accuracy and cannot rely on arbitrary guesses. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B); handoff: loan_advisory_agent_comprehensive_handoff.md § ZEN Engine behavior discovered |
| 734 | What does explanation faithfulness mean in credit decisions? | ANSWERED | Explanation faithfulness means the explanation must accurately reflect the factors the decision engine actually used – it must cite the exact failed rules, cover all reason/value elements, avoid ungrounded numbers, and be supported by the gold‑passage citations or counterfactual reasoning. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 3. Published metric families for the evaluation questions (s.44, s.45) |
| 735 | What does explanation completeness mean? | ANSWERED | Explanation completeness means that the explanation contains every failed ZEN reason and its associated value. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44) |
| 736 | What does explanation stability mean? | PARTIAL | Explanation stability means that the explanation (the ZEN trace) does not change for identical inputs and policy versions, achieved by stripping non‑deterministic fields so the trace hashes identically. | `decision_engine/evidence/evidence_record.py` |
| 737 | How should explanation quality be measured? | ANSWERED | Explanation quality is measured with concrete metrics such as explanation completeness (reason_value_coverage), faithfulness (citation support and strict citation correctness), and hallucination/unsupported‑claim rates, as defined in the evaluation harness. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 3. Published metric families for the evaluation questions (s.44, s.45) |
| 738 | Can existing XAI techniques support our use case? | ANSWERED | Existing XAI techniques like SHAP or LIME are not needed for our rule‑based decision engine and would require lender‑validated models, which we do not have; therefore they cannot directly support our current use case. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code) |
| 739 | What gaps remain between XAI research and applicant-facing explanations? | ANSWERED | The project acknowledges that while XAI research offers methods (SHAP, LIME, counterfactuals), it still lacks a real lender ML model and validated reason‑code list, and has not conducted user research to assess fairness or understandability of explanations, leaving a gap between research and applicant‑facing explanations. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § 2. How this applies to the project (decisions already evident in the code); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |

## 40. REGULATION / COMPLIANCE

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 740 | What Indian regulations apply to communicating loan rejection reasons? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 741 | What RBI requirements apply to NBFCs? | ANSWERED | RBI requires NBFCs to put in place an internal control system with designated compliance officers, adopt a board‑approved Fair Practices Code covering KYC, disclosure of loan terms, regulation of interest rates and communication of interest‑rate changes, publish the code on their website, and follow the 2025 Responsible Business Conduct directions on loan communication, grievance redressal and other operational safeguards. | policy source RBI-FPC-NBFC-2015 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=9823&Mode=0>; policy source RBI-RBC-NBFC-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/362MD26CA543937BA439A97E1BCFC08CF5808.PDF>; policy source RBI-NBFC-CF-2025 (approved) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_ViewMasDirections.aspx?id=12957> |
| 742 | What RBI requirements apply to digital lenders? | ANSWERED | RBI’s Digital Lending Directions require digital lenders to (i) provide on their DLA a link to the RE’s website with product, lender, LSP, customer‑care, privacy‑policy and Sachet‑Portal details; (ii) appoint a nodal grievance‑redressal officer whose details are prominently displayed; (iii) collect and store data in compliance with the Directions, keeping all data on servers located in India; (iv) report all lending through DLAs to Credit Information Companies; and (v) report every DLA deployed or discontinued to RBI’s Centralised Information Management System (CIMS) portal. For NBFCs, the RBI‑NBFC‑Credit‑Facilities Directions (2025) supersede the Digital Lending Directions and impose the same obligations. | policy source RBI-DLD-2025 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12848&Mode=0> |
| 743 | What requirements apply specifically to rejection communication? | ANSWERED | RBI Fair Practices Code mandates that banks must convey the main reason(s) for a loan rejection in writing, but NBFCs have no such clause; Bajaj Finance (an NBFC) only sends an SMS notifying the rejection. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection; `data/sources/policy_register.yaml`; run report: out/loan-bajaj/RESULTS.md § 1. Source register v2: 38 sources (verified 2026-10-14) |
| 744 | What information must be communicated to applicants? | ANSWERED | Applicants must be given a specific statement of the principal reason(s) for a rejection, together with the decision, the rule checks that led to it and the values they submitted, while internal scores or staff details are not disclosed. | `docs/research/xai-and-reason-codes.md` § Reason codes / adverse-action reasons (US comparison: ECOA / Regulation B); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security |
| 745 | When must rejection reasons be communicated? | ANSWERED | Banks must convey the main rejection reasons in writing (as required by RBI‑RBC‑CB para 331), whereas NBFCs have no written‑reasons clause; Bajaj Finance, for instance, only notifies rejection via SMS. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection |
| 746 | Are there restrictions on how reasons can be communicated? | ANSWERED | Yes. RBI rules require banks to convey the main rejection reasons in writing (RBC‑CB para 331), while NBFCs have no equivalent written‑reasons clause, so the agent must not claim a written reason is mandatory for NBFCs. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Reasons for rejection; run report: out/loan-bajaj/RESULTS.md § 1. Source register v2: 38 sources (verified 2026-10-14) |
| 747 | What information must not be disclosed? | UNKNOWN | The project does not define any specific information that must not be disclosed; whether a lender’s internal scoring model is confidential must be answered by the partner lender. **Who must answer:** a partner lender |  |
| 748 | Are internal scoring models protected from disclosure? | UNKNOWN | The project does not contain a real internal scoring model and no RBI source addresses disclosure; whether a lender’s model is confidential must be answered by the partner lender. **Who must answer:** a partner lender |  |
| 749 | What privacy requirements apply to applicant data? | ANSWERED | Applicant data must be collected on a need‑basis with the borrower’s prior explicit consent and an audit trail, stored with access controls, encryption and industry‑standard security, retained only as required by law, and deletable on request. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 3. Privacy (s.20) and data security; `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § RBI (Digital Lending) Directions, 2025 (May 8, 2025); policy source BFL-PRIVACY (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/privacy-policy> |
| 750 | What data-consent requirements apply? | ANSWERED | The regulator requires that data collection be need‑based and obtained only with the borrower’s prior, explicit consent that is auditable; borrowers must be able to give or deny consent for each data type, restrict third‑party sharing, set retention limits, and revoke consent, with the purpose of each collection disclosed at every interface stage. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § RBI (Digital Lending) Directions, 2025 (May 8, 2025); policy source RBI-DLD-2025 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12848&Mode=0>; policy source RBI-NBFC-CF-2025 (approved) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_ViewMasDirections.aspx?id=12957> |
| 751 | What data-retention requirements apply? | ANSWERED | RBI’s Digital Lending Directions require lenders to define and disclose their own data‑storage policy and do not set a fixed retention period; retention must comply with applicable Indian law, such as Bajaj Finance’s practice of keeping KYC documents for the duration of the relationship and at least five years after it ends. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § RBI (Digital Lending) Directions, 2025 (May 8, 2025); policy source BFL-PRIVACY (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/privacy-policy> |
| 752 | What requirements apply to automated decision-making? | ANSWERED | The RBI Digital Lending Directions contain no specific requirement for automated decision‑making, model explainability, or AI; they only address data storage and retention. Design references from other jurisdictions (e.g., EU GDPR Art. 22 and the EU AI Act) impose rights to human intervention and classify credit‑worthiness AI as high‑risk, which the project mirrors in its human‑review handoff design. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § RBI (Digital Lending) Directions, 2025 (May 8, 2025); `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 2. Design references from other jurisdictions (s.40 "useful as design references", s.41, s.22) |
| 753 | What requirements apply to explainability? | ANSWERED | The RBI Digital Lending Directions 2025 contain no specific requirement for model explainability or how rejection reasons must be worded, so there are no regulatory explainability obligations defined for Indian lenders. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § RBI (Digital Lending) Directions, 2025 (May 8, 2025) |
| 754 | What requirements apply to grievance redressal? | ANSWERED | Grievance redressal must be handled by designated nodal grievance officers whose contact details are prominently displayed on the RE’s and LSP’s websites, with a facility to lodge complaints, resolution within up to 10 working days at each escalation level and escalation to the RBI Ombudsman if not resolved within 30 days. | policy source RBI-DLD-2025 (approved_historical) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/NotificationUser.aspx?Id=12848&Mode=0>; policy source RBI-NBFC-CF-2025 (approved) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_ViewMasDirections.aspx?id=12957>; policy source BFL-GRIEVANCE (approved) <https://www.bajajfinserv.in/grievance-redressal> |
| 755 | What circumstances require human intervention? | ANSWERED | Human intervention is required when a data dispute is raised, when the loan system (Frappe) is unreachable, when the application is in manual review, or when an override/record edit or misrepresentation refusal occurs. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22) |
| 756 | What compliance obligations apply to AI-generated financial guidance? | ANSWERED | AI‑generated financial guidance must obey RBI data‑localisation and outsourcing rules (store data on Indian servers and retain full liability for any service‑provider), provide customer guidance and a human‑review hand‑off as required by RBI customer‑service directives and EU GDPR Art 22, and be designed so that the explainer is not classified as a high‑risk credit‑scoring system under the EU AI Act (only the deterministic underwriting engine falls under that regime). | policy source RBI-NBFC-CF-2025 (approved) <https://www.rbi.org.in/Scripts/BS_ViewMasDirections.aspx?id=12957>; `docs/research/frappe-schema-discovery.md` § E–G. Employment / financial / obligations; `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 2. Design references from other jurisdictions (s.40 "useful as design references", s.41, s.22) |
| 757 | Which regulations are relevant specifically in India? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 758 | Which regulations from other jurisdictions are useful as design references? | ANSWERED | The project cites EU GDPR Art. 22, the EU AI Act (Annex III 5(b)), and US ECOA/Regulation B with CFPB Circular 2022‑03 as useful design references from other jurisdictions. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § 2. Design references from other jurisdictions (s.40 "useful as design references", s.41, s.22) |
| 759 | What claims can the system safely make? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify which claims the system is permitted to make to users or regulators. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 760 | What claims should the system never make? | ANSWERED | The system must never claim it can guarantee approval, approve or reverse a loan decision, fabricate or hide information, disclose internal scoring or decision details, obey prompt‑injection commands, or submit/re‑apply on the applicant’s behalf. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 4. Safety edge cases (s.35) |

## 41. FAIRNESS / BIAS

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 761 | Could the system treat applicants differently based on irrelevant characteristics? | ANSWERED | The system does not treat applicants differently on irrelevant characteristics; decisions are deterministic ZEN rules on the canonical schema, and only explanation language or literacy level may vary. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41); `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 4. Quality, leakage and bias (measured) |
| 762 | Which applicant attributes could introduce unfairness? | ANSWERED | Attributes such as zip code, state, free‑text employment title/description, personal status, sex, age, foreign‑worker status, education and marital status could introduce unfairness if used in the model. | `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 4. Quality, leakage and bias (measured) |
| 763 | Should locality affect communication? | ANSWERED | Yes. Communication must be localized – it should be delivered in the borrower’s regional language or in the language the borrower selects, as required by RBI Responsible Business Conduct Directions. | policy source RBI-RBC-NBFC-2025 (approved) <https://rbidocs.rbi.org.in/rdocs/notification/PDFs/362MD26CA543937BA439A97E1BCFC08CF5808.PDF>; `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 764 | Should education affect communication? | PARTIAL | The prototype allows the wording of explanations to vary by the applicant‑chosen literacy level (and language), but it does not explicitly tie education to communication nor provide evidence that education should affect it. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 765 | Should occupation affect communication? | ANSWERED | No – the system’s design only varies communication by language and the applicant‑chosen literacy level, not by occupation. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 766 | Could communication adaptation accidentally become discriminatory? | PARTIAL | The project varies answer language and literacy level and includes an A2 paired test that compares low‑vs‑high‑literacy explanations, but it does not measure whether such adaptation leads to discriminatory outcomes; real‑applicant research would be needed. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 767 | Could different wording create different outcomes? | ANSWERED | In the built system the loan decision is fixed by ZEN rules before the agent generates explanations, and the agent cannot alter it, so different wording cannot change the decision outcome; any effect on applicant understanding or later behavior is not measured and would need real‑user research. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 768 | Could the agent give better advice to applicants who communicate more clearly? | PARTIAL | The system can vary answer language and literacy level and includes an A2 test comparing low vs high literacy, but it has not measured whether clearer communication yields better advice; user research is needed. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 769 | How do we prevent that? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not provide a concrete method for preventing fairness or bias issues; they only note existing bias measurements and limited testing (e.g., literacy adaptation) and state that further user research is needed. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 770 | Could financial-literacy estimation introduce bias? | PARTIAL | The project notes that the system adapts explanation language based on an estimated literacy level, but it has not measured whether this introduces bias in understanding or outcomes; only a paired test of low vs high literacy for the same persona is performed, without assessing protected attributes. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 771 | How do we evaluate fairness in explanations? | PARTIAL | Fairness in explanations is evaluated by the A2 literacy‑pair test, which compares low‑vs‑high‑literacy answers for the same persona and measures readability/jargon differences plus a judge rating; it does not assess protected attributes. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41); `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 6. Evaluation metrics the harness already computes (s.44) |
| 772 | Should all applicants receive the same factual information? | ANSWERED | No – the system tailors factual information to each applicant’s own recorded facts; only language and literacy level may differ, but the factual content reflects the individual’s data. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 773 | What can legitimately vary between applicants? | ANSWERED | The system only allows variation in the answer language, the applicant‑chosen literacy level, and the applicant’s own recorded facts; the underlying decision is fixed by ZEN rules and cannot be altered by the agent. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |
| 774 | How do we test whether personalization creates unequal treatment? | ANSWERED | The project tests personalization‑induced unequal treatment by using the evaluation harness’s A2 literacy‑pair set, which compares low‑vs‑high literacy explanations for the same persona as the closest built test. | `docs/research/regulation-and-design-references.md` § Fairness of explanations (s.41) |

## 42. DATASET DESIGN

| # | Question | Status | Answer (project as built) | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 775 | What data do we need to build a prototype? | ANSWERED | The prototype relies on synthetic data and policy files: a synthetic applicants JSON (8 cases), a synthetic policy JSON, and the ZEN decision model JSON, all mapped to the canonical schema. | `README.md` § Where things live |
| 776 | Does VaaniLabs.AI provide sample data? | ANSWERED | Yes. VaaniLabs.AI includes synthetic sample data such as `data/synthetic/applicants.json` with eight hand‑made cases and larger persona files. | `README.md` § Where things live; `docs/prototypes/p0-data-provenance.md` § P0 Data Provenance |
| 777 | If not, what public data can we use? | ANSWERED | The project can use public proxy datasets: primarily the US LendingClub data (Zenodo granting‑model extract and the Kaggle accepted‑file, with the rejected file pending a Kaggle token) and, as a fallback, the UCI Statlog German Credit dataset. Synthetic personas are used for any tests requiring Indian‑specific reason codes. | `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 1. Bottom line; `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 7. Recommendation |
| 778 | What synthetic data do we need? | ANSWERED | The project requires a synthetic policy file, a small hand‑crafted applicant fixture (8 cases), and a larger synthetic persona dataset that covers Indian income levels, FOIR, CIBIL scores, tenure, reason codes, and boundary, missing and invalid cases as well as applicant literacy and background. | `README.md` § Where things live; `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 6. Gaps that need synthetic scenarios or the partner |
| 779 | How realistic should synthetic applicant records be? | ANSWERED | Synthetic applicant records are not intended to mimic the statistical distribution of real borrowers; they should be realistic enough to cover rule variations, boundary, missing and invalid cases needed for testing, but not aim for full population realism. | `docs/prototypes/p0-data-provenance.md` § P0 Data Provenance; PR #2 (data/synthetic-personas): Phase 2: 2,560 synthetic applicant personas labelled by the P0/P1 pipeline § **Phase 2: synthetic applicant personas** (stacked 2/4, built on #1; merge #1 first. Diff vs `main` includes Phase 1 commits.); `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 6. Gaps that need synthetic scenarios or the partner |
| 780 | How should synthetic applicants be generated? | ANSWERED | Synthetic applicants should be generated with the `scripts/data/generate_personas.py` script, which creates ≥2,000 personas using a seed and draws attributes (income, FOIR, credit score, tenure, employment type, literacy, language, city tier, purpose) from defined distributions, then labels them via the unchanged P0/P1 pipeline using the synthetic‑v1 policy thresholds (income ≥ 25 000 INR, FOIR ≤ 0.45, score ≥ 700, tenure ≥ 12 months). | `scripts/data/generate_personas.py`; PR #2 (data/synthetic-personas): Phase 2: 2,560 synthetic applicant personas labelled by the P0/P1 pipeline § **Phase 2: synthetic applicant personas** (stacked 2/4, built on #1; merge #1 first. Diff vs `main` includes Phase 1 commits.); `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Files |
| 781 | How should synthetic rejection reasons be generated? | ANSWERED | Synthetic rejection reasons are generated by the persona generator script, which calls the unchanged `decide()` orchestrator (P0/P1 pipeline, ZEN 2.1.2) that applies the synthetic policy `personal_loan_synthetic_v1.json` to produce deterministic rejection reasons for each synthetic applicant. | `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Files; `README.md` § Where things live |
| 782 | How should synthetic policies be generated? | UNKNOWN | The materials do not specify the process for generating synthetic policies; they only note that synthetic policies exist as manually created prototype files. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 783 | How should applicant profiles be distributed across scenarios? | ANSWERED | The synthetic data is built so that each scenario contains at most four applicant profiles (including missing‑data cases, while invalid_data cases are excluded), ensuring a balanced distribution across scenarios. | `infra/frappe/bootstrap/make_seed.py` |
| 784 | How should edge cases be represented? | ANSWERED | Edge cases should be represented in the synthetic dataset as explicit rows covering exact boundary values, just‑below‑boundary values, missing values and invalid values for each field. | `docs/data/synthetic_personas.md` § Coverage (measured) |
| 785 | How should negative cases be represented? | ANSWERED | Negative cases should be represented by synthetic, rule‑based fixtures that each trigger a single‑rule failure, multi‑rule failures, exact boundary conditions, missing or invalid data, alongside all‑pass cases. | `docs/prototypes/p0-data-provenance.md` § P0 Data Provenance |
| 786 | How should refusal cases be represented? | ANSWERED | Refusal cases should be represented only with the synthetic persona dataset (Phase 2), using synthetic reason‑code fields rather than any real‑world rejection records. | `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 5. What each source can support; `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 6. Gaps that need synthetic scenarios or the partner |
| 787 | How should human-handoff cases be represented? | ANSWERED | Human‑handoff cases are represented by a handoff record that includes a generated reference (e.g., HO‑xxxxxx), the handoff category, related application references, a summary of the issue and a timestamp; the API response also carries `action="handoff"` and the `handoff_ref`. | `docs/research/design-decisions-in-code.md` § 2. Human handoff (s.22); `apps/api/src/loan_advisory/agent/tools.py` |
| 788 | How do we prevent synthetic data from accidentally representing real people? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** the project team |  |
| 789 | What licenses apply to public datasets? | ANSWERED | The public datasets listed are covered by CC‑BY‑4.0 (e.g., LendingClub Zenodo extract, UCI German Credit, Taiwan) and CC0‑1.0 (LendingClub Kaggle accepted mirror). | `data/sources/datasets.yaml` |
| 790 | What licenses apply to generated data? | UNKNOWN | Not answered by the project materials. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 791 | How should data provenance be documented? | ANSWERED | Data provenance is recorded in a licence/provenance register that lists each dataset’s source, licence, verification details, download status, and checksum (e.g., md5), together with any provenance caveats. | `data/sources/datasets.yaml`; `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 2. Candidates, licence and provenance; `docs/prototypes/p0-data-provenance.md` § P0 Data Provenance |
| 792 | How should training/build data be separated from evaluation data? | UNKNOWN | The provided materials do not specify a method for separating training/build data from evaluation data; the project team must define this split. **Who must answer:** VaaniLabs / the project team |  |
| 793 | How do we prevent evaluation leakage? | ANSWERED | We prevent evaluation leakage by explicitly identifying post‑decision columns and dropping them from any decision‑time experiment, using synthetic personas for reason‑code tasks, and ensuring training and test splits (including abstain sets) are disjoint. | `docs/data/dataset_audit.md` § 4. Quality, leakage and bias (measured); `scripts/data/audit_datasets.py` |

## 43. EVALUATION DATASET
